Untouched open Tk bug tickets: Reproduced

Triage of 548 open Bug tickets: those with no comment or check-in from me on 2026-09-24, plus 38 added on 2026-10-04 (filed since then, Pending then, or reopened; 16 of them filed by me), plus 68 added on 2026-10-22 (all other open or Pending Bug tickets not filed by me: the 30 created before 8.5, 31 I had already worked on, and 7 filed or missed since 10-04). Reproduced on Linux/X11 under Xvfb (main 9.1 tip, 9.0, 8.7, 8.6 tip, 8.5, 8.4) and on a Windows 11 VM (9.1, 8.6, 8.5, 8.4). Rows marked patch link to a prototype patch (150 in all, in tk-triage/prototypes). Updated 2026-10-31 with what happened since: the emoji before each ticket id shows its progress. On 2026-10-24 every prototype for a still-open ticket was verified again on current main, on Linux and Windows, and given a test where practical; on 2026-10-25 seven more reproduced tickets got prototypes.

💬8f2fdde1d22026ttk::treeview: setting a padding on a tag makes focus ring visible againpatchREPRODUCED (main)

Open ticket 8f2fdde1d2 · 88. Themed Tk · filed 2026-10-19

💬 Since triage (updated 2026-10-31)

Status now: Open

  • 💬 2026-10-20 bohagan: A custom style isn’t needed. From the docs all you need is: ttk::style configure Row -focusthickness 0 ttk::style map Row -focusthickness {} The padding issue is a ttk issue since 9.0. See the last few comments in [forum](https://core.tcl-lang.org/tips/forumpost/caa6535943).
Full verdict

REPRODUCED (main)

  • Platform generic (seen on X11, default theme; 9.1 only, because the Row layout gained Treeitem.focus with TIP 740)
  • Tested main/X11 with Xvfb screenshots. With ttk::style configure Row -focusthickness 0; ttk::style map Row -focusthickness {} and a tag with -padding {10 6}, the focused padded row shows a 1px black ring. An unpadded focused row shows none. 9.0 is not affected (its Row layout has no focus element).
  • Cause generic/ttk/ttkElements.c:DrawFocusRing raises thickness 0 to 1 when -focussolid is set, and all themes set -focussolid 1 for Row (the win theme uses a dotted ring, which ignores the thickness entirely). So the focus element always draws a 1px ring. Without padding, the Treeitem.row background inside it paints over the ring. A tag's -padding insets that background (Treeitem.padding) and the ring shows. FocusElementSize already reserves 0 pixels for thickness 0, so the drawing does not match the sizing.
  • Prototype 8f2fdde1d2.patch (34 lines; FocusElementDraw draws nothing when the scaled -focusthickness is < 1, and the clamp in DrawFocusRing is removed). Verified: the ring disappears for the padded row, and the default ring (focus → thickness 1) is still drawn. treeview, treetags, ttk, checkbutton, radiobutton, toggleswitch, entry, notebook and combobox tests pass. No new test (it would need pixel inspection).
  • Suggested ticket comment The focus element drew a 1px ring even with -focusthickness 0 (DrawFocusRing raised the thickness to 1 for solid rings). Normally the row background covers that ring, but tag -padding insets the background. Proposed fix: draw no ring when -focusthickness is 0, which matches the element's size computation. Separately, a tag's -padding does not grow the row height, so the padded rows' text is clipped (see the forum thread bohagan mentions). That is a different issue.
Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Linux
  • Status VERIFIED
  • Applies to main clean
  • Repro the focused row with tag -padding {10 6} and Row -focusthickness 0, checked with a PIL screen grab of the row's top edge. Before: 196 dark pixels. After: 0.
  • Test none (only pixels change; core Tk on X11 has no photo -format window)
  • Test file run treeview.test 1552/0, ttk.test 113/0, treetags.test 32/0, notebook.test 64/0 (same as main)
  • Patch changes none. Review: FocusElementDraw is the only caller of DrawFocusRing, and every theme maps Row -focusthickness to 1 when focused, so default rings are still drawn. With thickness 0, the dotted (non-solid) ring is now skipped too, which matches FocusElementSize reserving 0 px.
Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Windows VM, generic patches
  • 8f2fdde1d2: no test; ttk tests 0 failures.

  • 💬 2026-10-07 0xjnml: Confirmed with Orca 48.1 on a MATE desktop at 30d3e9a7b7: tabbing through the entry, check, radio and ttkbut demos of the widget demo, each control is announced once ("Dismiss button.", "Safety Check check box not checked."), where stock 9.1.0 announces each one twice. The script and the details are on 74d973b49d. As Serhiy noted, the window still has no parent: an AT-SPI client reads the parent of the demo's to…
  • 💬 2026-10-11 kevin_walzer: Merged fixes to trunk.
Full verdict

REPRODUCED (main; 9.0 and 8.6 have no accessibility)

  • Platform X11
  • Tested Orca 50.2 (not the reporter's 48.1) run under dbus-run-session on Xvfb :101 with a private HOME and XDG_RUNTIME_DIR. The speechd Python module was replaced by a stub, so the run was silent and everything Orca would speak was read from its --debug-file. The app was repro.tcl with a non-blocking focus tour (focus -force every 3 s). On main every widget is spoken twice: 'Button' 'button.' 'Save.' x2, 'Entry' 'entry.' x2, ... An AT-SPI event dump (python3-gi Atspi) shows the reported active-descendant-changed after every focus event. Both 01.patch and my one-line prototype give a single announcement each.
  • Cause the real trigger is in unix/tkUnixAccessibility.c:RegisterToplevelWindow(). The toplevel accessible never gets a parent: atk_object_set_parent() is only called for non-toplevels, and the root lists the toplevels only through tk_get_n_children/tk_ref_child. Orca's log shows "[window] claims to have no parent" and "None is top-level object for [button]", so Orca sets its active window to None. Right after presenting the focus it clears its locus of focus ("Focus [button] is not in None"). The active-descendant-changed then emitted on the parent (tk_grab_focus, RegisterWidgetRecursive, UpdateAtkFocusChain, TkAtkAccessible_FocusHandler) sets the locus again, and Orca presents the widget a second time. With a parent on the toplevel, Orca ignores the active-descendant event as "existing locus of focus". 01.patch is still sensible, since the signal is meant for item containers, but it only hides the missing parent. The same missing parent also leaves Orca's flat review with no window, and the script falls back to "default".
  • Prototype 3b2c972f29.patch (17 lines, 1 code line: atk_object_set_parent(accessible, tk_root_accessible) for toplevels; no test, Tk has no AT-SPI test harness). With it Orca 50.2 announces each focus change once and uses the "Tk Application" script.
  • Suggested ticket comment With Orca 50.2 the second announcement comes from the toplevel accessible having no parent. Orca cannot find the window of the focus, drops its locus of focus after announcing, and the following active-descendant-changed sets it again. Setting the toplevel's parent to the application object in RegisterToplevelWindow() alone removes the doubling (01.patch also does). Proposed fix in branch <name>.
✅324059e3d02026X11 accessibility: checkbuttons have role "unknown" (the role table has "Checkbox", accessibility.tcl sets "Checkbutton")patchREPRODUCED (main; 9.0 and 8.6 have no accessibility)

Open ticket 324059e3d0 · (unused) · filed 2026-10-03

✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-31)

Status now: Closed (Fixed)

  • 💬 2026-10-17 kevin_walzer: Fixed and merged.
Full verdict

REPRODUCED (main; 9.0 and 8.6 have no accessibility)

  • Platform X11
  • Tested AT-SPI tree of repro.tcl via python3-gi Atspi (private bus, Xvfb :101). Main shows [unknown] name='Checkbutton', and with 03.patch [check box]. On X11 with a reader running, ttk::checkbutton is aliased to tk::checkbutton (accessibility.tcl), and the class fallback in GetAtkRoleForWidget finds no "Checkbutton" entry either. Also checked:
    • After the checkbutton is turned off, ATK still reports CHECKED, because the value becomes "not selected".
    • Two radiobuttons sharing one variable are both reported CHECKED on main.
    • tk_dialog with the Dialog mapping added shows [dialog].
  • Cause unix/tkUnixAccessibility.c roleMap lacks "Checkbutton" and "Dialog", the roles set by library/accessibility.tcl and documented in doc/accessible.n. Two related defects:
    • tk_ref_state_set() treats any value whose first character is not '0' as checked, and _announce_button_state/_updateselection store "selected"/"not selected".
    • The Radiobutton/TRadiobutton <Map> bindings store [%W cget -variable], the variable name, as the value, so every radiobutton starts as checked.
  • Prototype 324059e3d0.patch (30 lines: 03.patch plus {"Dialog", ATK_ROLE_DIALOG} and "not selected" treated as unchecked; no test). The tree shows [check box], CHECKED is cleared after unchecking, and the dialog is [dialog]. The radiobutton value is not fixed there.
  • Suggested ticket comment Confirmed; 03.patch is right, and "Dialog" needs an entry too (ATK_ROLE_DIALOG). Once the role is fixed, the checked state is wrong after unchecking ("not selected" counts as checked in tk_ref_state_set), and all radiobuttons start checked because their <Map> binding stores the variable name as the value.

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main; 9.0 and 8.6 have no accessibility)

    • Platform X11
    • Tested:
      • spd-say/espeak stand-ins that log and sleep 0.5 s: focusing the entry 508 ms, two keys 1022 ms, toggle 525 ms. Sent: "entry blank", "a", "b", "Enable selected".
      • Answer to Kevin's question: Orca 50.2 with default settings (org.gnome.Orca key-echo = true; character-echo and word-echo false) on an X11 session, with keys typed into the ttk::entry by XTEST on Xvfb :101, speaks 'a' and 'b' itself (Orca debug log). Tk sent the same 'a' and 'b' to spd-say, so the user hears every key twice.
      • Orca's key echo comes from the keyboard events, not from the widget, so it works in any toolkit. Character and word echo need text-changed events and a real AtkText, and Tk's AtkText is a stub (get_text NULL, character count 0).
      • With the prototype, Orca still echoes each key once, Tk no longer sends keys, and the same steps take 2/1/6 ms.
    • Cause library/accessibility.tcl:
      • ::tk::accessible::speak runs exec spd-say synchronously from the event loop. That also blocks the AT-SPI bus, which Tk serves from the same loop: during the blocking tour, Orca's queries timed out and it processed the events seconds late.
      • It calls check_screenreader (popen "pgrep -x orca") every time.
      • _handle_keypress speaks every printable key on X11.
    • Prototype 48b39019bd.patch (38 lines; no test). speak runs exec spd-say -- $text ... & with the espeak fallback (dropping the useless quote escaping; "--" protects texts starting with "-"), and _handle_keypress no longer speaks on X11. Word, focus and state speech and the per-call check are left as they are.
    • Suggested ticket comment (reply to kevin_walzer): Orca's "key echo" is on by default and works from the keyboard events, independent of the widget. With Orca 50.2 on X11, keys typed into a ttk::entry are echoed by Orca and then again by Tk's spd-say. Character and word echo would need a real AtkText, which is currently a stub. Running spd-say in the background (exec ... &) at least stops the event loop, and with it the AT-SPI replies, from blocking.

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main; 9.0 and 8.6 have no accessibility)

    • Platform generic in accessibility.tcl (not aqua); tested on X11
    • Tested:
      • Tab order of repro.tcl: with the reader stand-in .f.save .f.name .f.enable .f.status; without a reader .f.save .f.name .f.enable. The label's -takefocus becomes 1 after map.
      • Answer to Kevin's question: Orca 50.2 flat review, driven through Orca's D-Bus remote controller (ToggleFlatReviewMode, GoEnd, PresentLine, SayAll) while the focus stays on the Save button. With the toplevel parent fix, Orca enumerates the unfocused label ("4 zones", including [label 'Ready.']) but reads "empty", because Tk's AtkText reports 0 characters for every widget. With the prototype the label's text is exposed through AtkText, and GoEnd/PresentLine and SayAll speak "Ready." without the label ever taking focus.
      • Without the parent fix, Orca has no window and flat review is empty anyway.
    • Cause library/accessibility.tcl:_init runs $w configure -takefocus 1 for every widget it initialises, including Label/TLabel. unix/tkUnixAccessibility.c:tk_text_get_text()/tk_text_get_character_count() are stubs, so a reader's review commands get no text for static widgets. (The ATK state set already omits FOCUSABLE for labels, which matches leaving them out of the Tab order.)
    • Prototype 595d72d0a6.patch (142 lines; no test). _init no longer sets -takefocus for the Label role, and for labels AtkText returns the label text (get_text, character count, get_string_at_offset, character and range extents = the widget box). Tab order with a reader matches the order without one, and flat review reads the label (together with 3b2c972f29.patch). The other static classes (TProgressbar, Scrollbar, Canvas) are left as they are.
    • Suggested ticket comment (reply to kevin_walzer): Yes. Orca reads static text with flat review and "read window" by walking the accessible tree, not through the focus. With Orca 50.2 the unfocused label is found, but read as "empty" because Tk's AtkText returns no text. Exposing the label text through AtkText makes flat review say "Ready." with the focus left on the Save button, so -takefocus 1 is not needed for labels.

    ✅e8d4c8a5772026X11 (HarfBuzz/SheenBidi font backend): [font configure] of a font in use leaks the old fontpatchREPRODUCED (main 40.3 KB per font change; 9.0.5 and 8.6.18 0.0 KB, they use tkUnixRFont.c)

    Open ticket e8d4c8a577 · (unused) · filed 2026-09-29

    ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-31)

    Status now: Closed (Fixed)

    • 🔧 2026-10-04 check-in 7ae80eeb69 on x11-bidi-font-reconfigure-leak, in main, 9.0: Fix e8d4c8a577: X11 (HarfBuzz/SheenBidi font backend): [font configure] of a font in use leaks the old font. TkpGetFontFromAttributes re-initialized a font in
    • 🔧 2026-10-06 check-in 13c5fdc004 on trunk, in main, 9.0: Fix e8d4c8a577: X11 (HarfBuzz/SheenBidi font backend): [font configure] of a font in use leaks the old font
    • 💬 2026-10-04 serhiy.storchaka: Proposed fix in branch x11-bidi-font-reconfigure-leak. It also fixes two related bugs: the fallback to "sans" destroyed the pattern a second time, and InitFont() used the font id to detect a re-used font record, so a failed re-initialization would have freed everything twice.
    • 💬 2026-10-06 serhiy.storchaka: Merged into main 13c5fdc004. Tk 9.0 is not affected.
    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main 40.3 KB per font change; 9.0.5 and 8.6.18 0.0 KB, they use tkUnixRFont.c)

    • Platform X11 (only the bidi font backend, unix/tkUnixBidiFont.c, which is the configure default on main)
    • Tested the ticket's reproducer (1500 font configure of a named font used by a label) under Xvfb :101 with build-trunk, tk9.0 and tk8.6. With the prototype: 0.0 KB per change. valgrind on the patched build (150 changes): no invalid reads or writes, 512 bytes "definitely lost" not from the font code. font.test, unixFont.test and fontchooser.test pass (same counts as unpatched main).
    • Cause unix/tkUnixBidiFont.c:TkpGetFontFromAttributes() re-initializes a live TkFont in place (UpdateDependentFonts -> TkpGetFontFromAttributes(fontPtr,...)) by calling InitFont() directly, without the FinishedWithFont() that tkUnixRFont.c calls first. InitFont() then overwrites faces[], the FcFontSet, the FcPattern, the Xft/HarfBuzz objects, the charsets and the shaper's hb_buffer. Also confirmed in the code: (1) on the fallback path TkpGetFontFromAttributes() calls XftPatternDestroy(pattern) on a pattern that InitFont() has already destroyed on every failure path (directly after FcFontSort, or through FinishedWithFont via fontPtr->pattern), which is a double free; (2) InitFont() uses fontPtr->font.fid != 0 to mean "re-used record". A new font that fails after XLoadFont() is therefore never freed. Once FinishedWithFont() is added, a re-used record that fails is left with dangling faces/pattern/fontset pointers, and TkpDeleteFont() would free them a second time.
    • Prototype e8d4c8a577.patch (90 lines, no test: a leak test would need memory accounting). It calls FinishedWithFont() before the in-place InitFont(), makes FinishedWithFont() clear the pointers and fid it frees, replaces the fid test with an explicit reused flag, and drops the double XftPatternDestroy(). Font tests pass and the reproducer is flat.
    • Suggested ticket comment Confirmed on main; 9.0 and 8.6 are not affected. Besides the missing FinishedWithFont(), the fallback's XftPatternDestroy() is indeed a double free, and InitFont() must not use font.fid to detect a re-used record (a failed re-init would otherwise free everything twice). Proposed fix in branch <name> (all three).

    • Platform see title
    • Tested filed after reproducing it; see the ticket
    • Prototype none
    Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Linux
    • Status VERIFIED
    • Applies to main ported. Main restructured init: TkAtkAccessibility_Init now only calls TkWaylandAccessibility_Init, and there is a new ::tk::accessible::speak command backed by global speechd state (spd_conn, pending message, Tcl timer). The thread check now lives in TkAtkAccessibility_Init before that call. Non-ATK threads also get a no-op ::tk::accessible::speak, because the speechd state is process-wide too. As a result, SpeechExitProc is registered only once.
    • Configuration main configures with accessibility enabled by default ("checking for ATK and Atk-bridge... yes", HAVE_ATK=1, HAVE_SPEECHD=1).
    • Repro tclsh loads Thread 3.0.7 (a copy of work-a1's build). N threads each run load libtcl9tk9.1.so Tk, then create, pack and destroy 50 frames, then destroy ..
      • Unpatched main, 4 threads: 2/6 runs crashed (SIGSEGV after "GLib-GObject-CRITICAL ... invalid (unclassed) pointer"; abort "double free or corruption").
      • Patched, 4 threads: 0/25 and 0/6 runs crashed. 8 threads: 0/40.
      • One earlier patched run (1/20, 4 threads) aborted with "double free or corruption (!prev)". The old non-ATK multi-thread crash from the ticket explains it: a --disable-accessibility build of the same main crashes 3/40 at 8 threads ("malloc(): unaligned tcache chunk detected"). That bug is separate and not addressed here.
      • Under gdb, one run hung for 20 minutes (vfork children). I killed it and did not get a backtrace.
    • Test none. It needs the Thread package, which the Tk test suite does not depend on. The crash is probabilistic, and the older non-ATK heap corruption would make such a test flaky anyway.
    • Test file run tk.test 38/0, safe.test 16/0 (same as main; there is no dedicated accessibility test file)
    • Patch changes ported as described above (+speak no-op). Backup: 97a7ddac25-atk-thread.patch.1024.

    • 💬 2026-10-24 serhiy.storchaka: This is a race: wm withdraw unmaps the toplevel at once, but its window manager frame stays mapped until the window manager handles the unmap, and Tk_CoordsToWindow() still returned the toplevel. With a slow window manager, after 10 is not enough. Fixed on branch x11-coords-to-window (8a0667c7fc): a toplevel which is not mapped is not returned. New test winfo-4.9 has no delay; without the fix it failed under ope…
    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 9.0, 8.6) as a timing race. winfo-4.8 itself passes here with no WM and with twm/fvwm/icewm/openbox/wmaker/i3/xfwm4, but it fails whenever the WM is slower than after 10.

    • Platform X11 (the #591829e948-B fix in check-in 5261b9cd44 changed only macosx/tkMacOSXWm.c)
    • Tested main, 9.0, 8.6 on Xvfb. A probe calls winfo containing right after wm withdraw .one; update. It returns .one on 9.0+fvwm, main/9.0+icewm, and main/9.0/8.6+openbox. After 10 ms it returns "", and winfo ismapped .one is already 0.
    • Cause unix/tkUnixWm.c Tk_CoordsToWindow. The toplevel wrapper is unmapped at once, but the WM's decoration frame (wmPtr->reparent) stays mapped until the WM handles the UnmapNotify. XTranslateCoordinates still finds the frame, and the code accepts that toplevel without checking whether it is mapped.
    • Prototype 586f722cdd.patch (23 lines). After gotToplevel it returns NULL if !Tk_IsMapped(winPtr). It adds test winfo-4.9, which uses no delay after the withdraw. winfo-4.9 fails without the fix under openbox/icewm and passes with it. winfo.test has no new failures: with no WM, winfo-9.4/9.7 fail both before and after the patch; under wmaker, winfo-4.5 fails both before and after.
    • Suggested ticket comment On X11 this is a race. After wm withdraw the WM frame stays mapped until the WM reacts, and Tk_CoordsToWindow accepts the frame without checking that the toplevel is mapped. A fix and a test that doesn't depend on timing (winfo-4.9) are ready.
    ⬜3249f7d4c62026MS-Win: canvas does not rotate text if font replacedpatchREPRODUCED (9.1 main); NOT-REPRODUCED (8.6 tip)

    Open ticket 3249f7d4c6 · 05. Canvas Items · filed 2026-04-21

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (9.1 main); NOT-REPRODUCED (8.6 tip)

    • Platform Windows
    • Tested code review. Repro script: win/3249f7d4c6.tcl (Courier and Courier New side by side, -angle 90, with bbox rectangles).
    • Cause (likely): c0dbdd3ff3 (3f19aebf80, 2017-01-28) was merged to 9.0 but not 8.6. It removed the hack in win/tkWinFont.c FamilyExists that rejected "Courier", "Times" and "Helvetica". In 9.0, "Courier" can therefore resolve to the GDI raster font (coure.fon). GDI ignores lfEscapement/lfOrientation for raster fonts, so GetScreenFont(angle) returns an unrotated font while the bbox is rotated. Possible fix: make WinFontExistProc reject fontType & RASTER_FONTTYPE, so the fallback picks a TrueType font. Alternatively, create angled fonts with OUT_TT_ONLY_PRECIS.
    • Prototype none (Windows-only; confirm first)
    • Suggested ticket comment Probably "Courier" now maps to the raster Courier font, which GDI cannot rotate. That is a side effect of c0dbdd3ff3 no longer rejecting Courier/Times/Helvetica. Rejecting raster fonts in WinFontExistProc (or using OUT_TT_ONLY_PRECIS for angled fonts) should fix it. Harald, please run the attached script.
    Windows VM, run 1
    • Platform Windows
    • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main); NOT-REPRODUCED (8.6 tip)
    • Tested automated screen-capture repro (work-win/fontrot.tcl + fontrot.ps1: measures the ink bbox of "ABCDEF" at -angle 90).

    9.1: font actual gives Courier, and the text is drawn unrotated (ink 53x10). Courier New, Times and Helvetica are rotated.

    8.6: Courier resolves to Courier New and is rotated.

    • Cause 3f19aebf80 (2017-01-28, fix for c0dbdd3ff3) removed the Courier/Times/Helvetica rejection from win/tkWinFont.c:FamilyExists. On Windows, "Courier" now matches the raster coure.fon, and GDI cannot rotate raster fonts (lfEscapement is ignored). Explicitly chosen raster fonts such as Fixedsys and Terminal are also unrotated. That is inherent to GDI.
    • Prototype 3249f7d4c6.patch (16 changed lines). FamilyExists passes a flag to WinFontExistProc that skips RASTER_FONTTYPE entries, but only for families that have a Tk alias list (TkFontGetAliasList: Courier/Times/Helvetica/...). "Courier" therefore falls back to "Courier New", while a real TrueType "Courier" (the case in c0dbdd3ff3) is still found. I relaxed test winfont-2.8 to accept {Courier New}. Verified: the Courier text is now rotated, and font.test, winFont.test and canvText.test pass (0 failures). Before the test change, winfont-2.8 failed as expected.
    • Suggested ticket comment On Windows, "Courier" is the raster font coure.fon, which GDI cannot rotate. Since c0dbdd3ff3, Tk no longer replaces it with Courier New. A fix is to ignore raster fonts in FamilyExists for the aliased families (Courier/Times/Helvetica), so Courier falls back to Courier New again. A TrueType "Courier" is still used when installed.

    Open ticket 3ac12ff34c · 84. Unix Build · filed 2025-12-01

    ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-31)

    Status now: Closed (Fixed)

    • 🔧 2026-09-27 check-in f99d3c2cbf on configure-cups-cflags, in main, 9.0: Fix 3ac12ff34c: configure may not find cups.h. Add the output of "cups-config --cflags" to CUPS_CFLAGS, so cups.h is found in a non-default prefix, e.g. /usr/
    • 🔧 2026-09-27 check-in bd2f2ed155 on trunk, in main, 9.0: Fix 3ac12ff34c: configure may not find cups.h
    • 🔧 2026-09-27 check-in 94362ce151 on core-9-0-branch, in main, 9.0: Fix 3ac12ff34c: configure may not find cups.h
    • 💬 2026-09-27 serhiy.storchaka: Reproduced on OpenBSD 7.9 with the cups-libs package: "checking for cups/cups.h... no", and Tk is silently built without CUPS support. With cups-config --cflags added to CUPS_CFLAGS, cups.h is found and Tk links with libcups. Proposed fix in branch configure-cups-cflags. The OpenBSD port applies the same patch.
    • 💬 2026-09-27 jan.nijtmans: Fixed bd2f2ed155. Also backported to core-9-0-branch. Thanks for the report and the fix. And thanks for reminding!
    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 9.0; 8.6 has no CUPS code)

    • Platform build
    • Tested a fake cups-config whose --cflags returns -I<dir>, with cups/cups.h only in that dir. Unpatched main configure prints "checking for cups/cups.h... no" and leaves CUPS_CFLAGS empty. Patched configure prints "yes" and sets CUPS_CFLAGS = -DHAVE_CUPS -I<dir>. (Real libcups-dev is not installed here.)
    • Cause unix/configure.ac: CUPS_CFLAGS does not include cups-config --cflags, so AC_CHECK_HEADER and the build of tkUnixPrint.c miss non-default include dirs, e.g. /usr/local/include on BSD.
    • Prototype 3ac12ff34c.patch (configure.ac plus configure regenerated with autoconf 2.73; 1 line changed in each)
    • Suggested ticket comment Confirmed: CUPS_CFLAGS lacks cups-config --cflags, so cups.h in a non-default prefix is not found. The fix is to append it as suggested, for 9.0 and main.

    • 💬 2026-10-02 serhiy.storchaka: TCL_SRC_DIR is used because Tk normally builds against the Tcl source tree, and it never used TCL_INCLUDE_SPEC. Tk needs only the public Tcl headers. Proposed fixes in branches unix-tcl-installed-headers and win-tcl-installed-headers (MinGW): configure uses the installed Tcl headers if the Tcl source tree is not readable. The MSVC build (makefile.vc) still requires the Tcl sources.
    • 💬 2026-10-05 serhiy.storchaka: Backported to 8.6 in 6b825fa561.
    • 💬 2026-10-05 serhiy.storchaka: The Unix part was merged into main in 2e03afb4d0 and 9.0 in c0549801a3. The MinGW part in win-tcl-installed-headers is not merged yet.
    • 💬 2026-10-07 jan.nijtmans: The d80a5f1b14 is merged now too. Thanks! Closing
    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main; 8.6 has the same Makefile.in). With TCL_SRC_DIR pointing to a directory that is not readable, cc1 fails with "Permission denied" on -I$TCL_SRC_DIR/generic. When the directory does not exist, the build finds tcl.h only if the headers are on a default or CPATH include path, because Tk never uses TCL_INCLUDE_SPEC.

    • Platform build (unix)
    • Tested main, configured against a copy of an installed tclConfig.sh with TCL_SRC_DIR changed, in both cases (unreadable directory, missing directory)
    • Cause unix/Makefile.in uses -I${TCL_GENERIC_DIR} -I${TCL_PLATFORM_DIR} (from TCL_SRC_DIR) unconditionally and ignores TCL_INCLUDE_SPEC.
    • Prototype b900dc7552.patch (configure.ac + Makefile.in, 16 changed lines; unix/configure must be regenerated with autoconf 2.73). configure sets TCL_INCLUDES to the source-tree -I flags if $TCL_SRC_DIR/generic/tcl.h is readable, and to TCL_INCLUDE_SPEC otherwise. Verified: a full build succeeds with the unreadable source directory, and a build against tclmain/unix still uses the source tree.
    • Suggested ticket comment Tk always added the -I flags for the Tcl source tree and never used TCL_INCLUDE_SPEC. Proposed fix: configure uses the installed Tcl headers (TCL_INCLUDE_SPEC) when the Tcl source tree is not readable.

    • Cause unix/tkUnixSend.c:Tk_SetAppName() calls UpdateCommWindow() (XChangeProperty of TK_APPLICATION on the comm window) *after* RegClose(), which does the XFlush. The workaround comment in tests/testutils.tcl childTkProcess could be dropped later.
    • Suggested ticket comment On X11, Tk_SetAppName() updated the TK_APPLICATION property of the comm window only after the registry was closed and flushed, so another process could consider the name stale and take it over. Fix: update the property before RegClose(), with test send-17.2. On Windows, send is disabled (bug 858822).
    Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Linux
    • Status VERIFIED+TEST
    • Applies to main clean
    • Repro before send-17.2 gives the duplicate name (4 of 4 runs) / after {t_s_dup #2} {t_s_dup {t_s_dup #2}} t_s_dup (4 of 4 runs)

    Open ticket a58cda72fe · 68. Win Window Operations · filed 2025-07-24

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (9.1 main). The crash is GDI handle exhaustion.

    • Platform Windows
    • Tested not on Linux (no Thread package installed). Repro: win/a58cda72fe.tcl (driver runs child processes with 50 to 500 threads and prints a VERDICT line).
    • Cause xlib/ximage.c:XCreateBitmapFromData() does not check for Tk_GetPixmap() returning None, which win/tkWinPixmap.c does when CreateBitmap and CreateDIBSection both fail. TkPutImage() then gets drawable 0, which is a NULL TkWinDrawable in TkWinGetDrawableDC(). A fix would return None from XCreateBitmapFromData() when pix == None, and make GetBitmap() (tkBitmap.c) fail with an error instead of caching a None bitmap. Graceful degradation only: running out of GDI handles is an OS limit.
    • Prototype none (Windows-only, cannot verify)
    • Suggested ticket comment The crash is running out of GDI handles: Tk_GetPixmap() returns None and XCreateBitmapFromData() passes it on without checking. Tk could report an error instead of crashing, but the 10000-handle limit remains.
    Windows VM, run 2
    • Platform Windows
    • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main). The crash is GDI handle exhaustion.
    • Tested the VM has no Thread package, so I built tcltest91.exe/tcltest86.exe (the tcltest target in tcl91 and tcl8.6; only new build outputs were added) and used testthread (work-win/a58tt.tcl + a58.cmd). With the ref91 build, 50–500 threads were OK. 600 threads crashed with 0xC0000005 in TkWinGetDrawableDC(NULL): the per-process GDI count reached its 10000 limit.

    After fixing that NULL case, a second crash appeared: 0xC0000094 (integer divide by zero) in Tk_GetPixmap. A thread whose TkpOpenDisplay found GetDC(NULL) failing got planes=0 and depth=0 in TkWinDisplayChanged.

    With the prototype, 600, 800 and 1000 threads run without a crash. The extra threads get the Tcl error couldn't connect to display ":0" (721 of 1000).

    • Cause after the GDI quota runs out, Tk_GetPixmap() returns None. The crash I captured was widget double-buffering (TkpDisplayButton → XCopyArea → TkWinGetDrawableDC(None)): the NULL twdPtr is dereferenced. XCreateBitmapFromData also passes None on to TkPutImage. GetBitmap() caches a None bitmap, so a second failure would panic with "bitmap already registered". Also, TkWinDisplayChanged ignores a failed GetDC(NULL).
    • Prototype a58cda72fe.patch (about 60 changed lines incl. comments; same patch as f96c6eb36b). The changes:
      • TkWinGetDrawableDC/TkWinReleaseDrawableDC treat None as a NULL DC, so drawing into it does nothing.
      • XCreateBitmapFromData returns None if Tk_GetPixmap fails.
      • GetBitmap raises cannot create bitmap "gray25" (errorCode TK BITMAP CREATE).
      • TkWinDisplayChanged keeps the old data if GetDC(NULL) fails, and TkpOpenDisplay then returns NULL.
      • Photo: see f96c6eb36b.

    Verified on 9.1: bitmap, button, canvas, entry, font, frame, imgPhoto, imgBmap, image and listbox tests all pass (0 failures).

    • Suggested ticket comment Confirmed on Windows 11. With ~600 threads the process hits the 10000 GDI object limit. Tk_GetPixmap() then returns None, which is later dereferenced (TkWinGetDrawableDC), and a thread opening its display gets depth 0 (a divide by zero in Tk_GetPixmap). A patch that makes these cases fail gracefully (drawing into None does nothing, bitmaps give a Tcl error, new threads get "couldn't connect to display") is ready. The GDI limit itself remains.
    Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Windows VM
    • Status VERIFIED for the panic, with a performance caveat
    • Applies to main clean. It conflicts textually with 1567354fff in TkWinDisplayChanged.
    • Repro (f96c6eb36b.tcl, 12000 photo labels): before: the process aborts (0x80000003) after ~4750 images / after: all 12000 are created without a panic. But once the GDI quota is exhausted, every 250 images take 25–40 s instead of under 1 s. The instances retry Tk_GetPixmap on every redraw, which looks quadratic. The final update+exit did not finish within 20 min, and process teardown then took several minutes. The 600-thread repro (needs a tcltest build with testthread) was not re-run.
    • Test none (needs GDI quota exhaustion; this would break the test process)
    • Test file run B3: bitmap 7/0, imgPhoto 248/0, canvas 175/0, button 416/0, frame 207/0, winfo 76/0 (pre-existing failures: none)
    • Patch changes none. Suggest throttling the retry in TkImgPhotoInstanceSetSize/ImgPhotoDisplay, e.g. retry only after the size changes or after another image is freed.
    ⬜d94a3c7b422025Selection by words: difference between mouse selection & keyboard selectionREPRODUCED (main, 9.0); not in 8.6, 8.5 or 8.7b1 (no ICU word procs there)

    Open ticket d94a3c7b42 · 18. [text] · filed 2025-06-06

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 9.0); not in 8.6, 8.5 or 8.7b1 (no ICU word procs there)

    • Platform generic (X11 and Windows; any build with ICU available)
    • Tested X11 with "foo.bar baz". In text on main/9.0: Control-Shift-Left from after "bar" selects {foo.bar}, Control-Shift-Right from 1.0 selects {foo.bar}, double-click on "foo" selects {foo}. The entry behaves the same way. On 8.6/8.5/8.7b1 the keys select {bar} and {foo}, which matches double-click.
    • Cause generic/tkIcu.c replaces ::tk::startOfNextWord, ::tk::startOfPreviousWord and ::tk::endOfWord with ICU word-break iterators. ICU follows UAX#29, where "." between letters (MidNumLet) does not break a word. ::tk::wordBreakBefore/After, which double-click uses (text.tcl TextSelectTo, entry.tcl), are still the Tcl regex procs from library/tk.tcl that use tcl_wordchars (\w). The two sets of procs therefore disagree about what a word is.
    • Prototype none (this is a design choice: either add ICU-based ::tk::wordBreakBefore/After in tkIcu.c so double-click also uses UAX#29, or stop using ICU for word motion)
    • Suggested ticket comment Reproduced on X11 with 9.0/main but not 8.6. Keyboard word motion uses ICU (UAX#29, which does not split "foo.bar"), while double-click uses the regex-based ::tk::wordBreakBefore/After. We need to decide which definition to use and apply it to both.

    ⬜e94c8bc8452025X11 clipboard clear command has no effect when xclipboard is runningREPRODUCED (main, 8.6). This is largely inherent to X11 clipboard managers.

    Open ticket e94c8bc845 · 52. [clipboard] · filed 2025-05-23

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6). This is largely inherent to X11 clipboard managers.

    • Platform X11
    • Tested Xvfb, main and 8.6.18. xclipboard is not installed, so it was emulated by a Tk app that re-owns CLIPBOARD after fetching its content, which is what xclipboard does. After clipboard append aa followed by clipboard clear, both xsel -b and Tk's own clipboard get still return "aa"; a later append ("bb") is seen correctly. Without a manager, another client that asks for STRING after clipboard clear gets "CLIPBOARD selection doesn't exist or form STRING not defined".
    • Cause generic/tkClipboard.c Tk_ClipboardClear re-owns CLIPBOARD but has no targets. The manager then gets a conversion failure and keeps (and re-asserts) the old content. X11 has no protocol for "clipboard is now empty". One option is for clipboard clear to serve an empty STRING/UTF8_STRING, but real xclipboard may still ignore zero-length data (could not verify; xclipboard not installed).
    • Prototype none (behaviour change of questionable value; can't test against real xclipboard)
    • Suggested ticket comment Reproduced on X11 with an xclipboard-like manager. After clipboard clear, Tk owns CLIPBOARD with no targets, so the manager's fetch fails and it re-asserts the old content. X11 has no way to say "the clipboard is empty", so this looks like a limitation of the protocol rather than a Tk bug.

    • 💬 2026-10-17 nemethi: Backported to core-9-0-branch and core-8-6-branch by commits 0459709f and 1511f237. Closing the ticket.
    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6). The grid bookkeeping breaks; I did not see the pixel blow-up (0 or 32483 px) on X11.

    • Platform generic (tested on X11)
    • Tested main and 8.6 tip, with no WM, openbox and icewm. Panedwindow with 2 text -setgrid 1 panes. The toplevel was resized from outside with XResizeWindow on the wrapper, then the sash was moved with sash place or a B1 drag.

    After the resize, every sash move makes the reported grid geometry wrong: at 350 px, wm geometry goes from 26x11 to 15x11; later, 4x11 at 329 px. The toplevel keeps its pixel size here because openbox/icewm don't re-apply the changed hints. The WMs on Aqua/Win evidently do (Marc's report).

    Without -setgrid everything stays consistent. Main's widget demo no longer uses -setgrid: 0e800a5026 (main) and cafa154041 (9.0), 2025-03-20/21. So the demo itself no longer shows the bug.

    • Cause the sash code (tkPanedWindow.c) sets the panedwindow's requested width to its current width after a sash move. This changes the toplevel's reqWidth, but the gridded text's reqGridWidth stays the same. So the grid base (reqWidth - reqGridWidth*inc) in tkUnixWm.c UpdateGeometryInfo/UpdateSizeHints moves by the sash delta. The WM's width in grid units and the size hints then no longer describe the same pixel size. This is a -setgrid design problem, not a panedwindow bug; see the unmerged getrid_setgrid branch.
    • Prototype none (needs a decision on -setgrid, i.e. the getrid_setgrid branch or a TIP, not a small fix)
    • Suggested ticket comment Reproduced on X11 (main, 8.6): after a WM resize, each sash move shifts the grid base, and [wm geometry] then reports nonsense grid sizes. It only happens with a gridded pane (-setgrid 1). The widget demo no longer uses -setgrid (0e800a5026). The real fix depends on the getrid_setgrid branch.

    • 🔧 2026-10-23 check-in 423012d795 on x11-render-before-xft, not merged: Fix b298b92ba7: when Tk was loaded into one thread after another on X11, a later thread died with a random X error. Xft's close-display hook frees glyphs thro
    • 💬 2026-10-23 serhiy.storchaka: This is not a regression: it happens in all versions, 8.6.9 included, once each thread creates a widget with a font. The script in the report passes in 8.6 and 9.1 only because no font is used there. When a display is closed, the close hook of Xft frees its cached glyphs through RENDER after the close hook of RENDER has already run. That registers RENDER again for the dying display, and a new display opened later …
    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 9.1b1, 9.0.5, 8.7b1, 8.6 tip, 8.6.13, 8.6.9)

    • Platform X11 (Xft builds)
    • Tested Thread 3.0.7 / 2.8.13, sequential thread::create {package require Tk; ...} + thread::release, without a WM.

    With the reporter's exact script, 9.0.5 and 8.7b1 fail but main, 9.1b1 and 8.6 pass. Bisect says main started passing at ea378fca79 (2026-05-28, TIP 740 merge), but that only moved the first Xft call out of the init path.

    Once each thread creates a widget with a font (pack [label .l -text hi]; update), every version fails within 3–5 iterations with a random X error ("BadRequest", "BadLength") and the process exits. This happens even when each thread has fully exited before the next one starts, so it is not a thread race.

    • Cause gdb shows that Xft's close-display hook runs after RENDER's hook. When closing its cached fonts it calls XRenderFreeGlyphSet → XRenderFindDisplay. That re-registers RENDER for the dying Display*, with codes that are then freed. The next thread's XOpenDisplay gets the same address (confirmed) and inherits the stale entry, so RENDER requests go out with a garbage major opcode.

    Close hooks run in LIFO order. Workaround in unix/tkUnixEvent.c TkpOpenDisplay: call XRenderQueryExtension() right after opening the display, so RENDER is registered before Xft and its hook runs last.

    • Prototype b298b92ba7.patch (17 changed lines, no test because the test needs the Thread package). With it, 40 iterations × {label, ttk::label+text+canvas}, with and without waiting for thread exit, run clean. The unpatched control fails. unixFont/font/unixEmbed tests pass.

    Separate issue: the process often segfaults at exit (rc 139) in every version, including 8.6, if threads with Tk were released just before. Not investigated.

    • Suggested ticket comment Reproduced on X11 in all versions (8.6.9 too) once a thread creates a widget with a font. The cause is Xft's close hook calling RENDER after RENDER's own hook, which leaves a stale entry for the freed Display*; a later display at the same address then sends garbage opcodes. Initializing RENDER in TkpOpenDisplay before Xft fixes it (prototype available).

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    • Platform Windows
    • Tested main on Xvfb
    • Cause (likely): the Windows per-process GDI object quota (10000 by default). Each photo instance holds an HBITMAP. The failure count does not depend on image size, which points to a handle limit rather than memory. In win/tkWinPixmap.c:Tk_GetPixmap, CreateBitmap fails, then the GetDC(NULL) fallback fails too (ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER). The NULL result makes TkImgPhotoInstanceSetSize panic. Tk cannot raise the quota, so the realistic fix is to fail gracefully (a Tcl error or an empty instance) instead of calling Tcl_Panic.
    • Prototype none (Windows only). Repro script: win/f96c6eb36b.tcl. It writes its progress and the verdict to f96c6eb36b.log next to the script. After a panic, the last "created N" line shows how far it got.

    ⬜168f3ef1302024Going on words with Ctrl+arrow in text widgetpatchREPRODUCED (main, 9.0); 8.6 and 8.7 (no ICU) behave the old way

    Open ticket 168f3ef130 · 18. [text] · filed 2024-01-25

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 9.0); 8.6 and 8.7 (no ICU) behave the old way

    • Platform generic (Linux with ICU)
    • Tested main, 9.0, 8.7, 8.6 on Xvfb, with the ticket's snippet, pressing Control-Right/Left via event generate. Stops on main and 9.0:

    Ctrl-Right |set |::var1 |value!!|!!! / |set |::var2 |"$value |+ |$value2"

    Ctrl-Left |set |::|var1 |value|!!|!!|! / |set |::|var2 |"$|value |+ $|value2|"

    • Cause generic/tkIcu.c startEndOfCmd uses the ICU word iterator, which ends a segment after every punctuation character. It only checks whether the character after a boundary is whitespace (FLAG_SPACE), not the segment's rule status (ubrk_getRuleStatus, UBRK_WORD_NONE). Runs of punctuation such as !!!!! and :: therefore produce unpredictable stops, and the TextNextWord (endOfWord then startOfNextWord) and TextPrevPos steps pair up inconsistently. Since b4a14597, Ctrl+Right goes to the start of the next word on every platform.
    • Prototype none (behaviour choice: treat a run of punctuation as one word, or skip UBRK_WORD_NONE segments; this ties into the -locale "legacy" discussion; the text -locale option now exists on main, default "C")
    • Suggested ticket comment Still present on trunk. The ICU-based word search in tkIcu.c only special-cases whitespace, so every punctuation character counts as a word (!!|!!|!, $|value). Using the ICU rule status to group punctuation runs would give the consistent stops editors have.
    2026-10-25 prototype, Linux
    • Status PROTOTYPED
    • Cause generic/tkIcu.c startEndOfCmd uses the ICU word iterator, which puts a boundary after every punctuation character. The code only checks whether the character after a boundary is whitespace, so every !, : or $ counts as its own word. The single extra icu_next()/icu_previous() step then lands in the middle of a punctuation run (value!!|!!!, value!|!!|!!, :|=, y-|>z).
    • Fix new helper SkipPunctuation(). In word mode it is applied after every iterator move (following/preceding/next/previous). It skips a boundary when the segment before it has rule status UBRK_WORD_NONE (<100, via ubrk_getRuleStatus, a newly loaded symbol) and does not end in a space, and the character after it (surrogate pairs decoded) is neither a space nor Tcl_UniCharIsWordChar. A run of punctuation then counts as one word. Checking the rule status, and not only the characters, keeps the boundary after a letter plus a combining mark. If the symbol is missing, the old behaviour is kept. The ICU used is the system libicuuc.so.78, loaded at run time by Icu_Init; the normal --disable-bidi build already loads it (::tk::startOfNextWord is the C command, and cluster.test's needsICU constraint is true). The text widget's default -locale C goes through ICU; -locale tcl8 still uses the Tcl 8 procs and is unchanged (|set ::|var1 |value!!!!! before and after). 168f3ef130.patch (54 changed lines, 42 C + 12 test)
    • Repro stops found with event generate <Control-Right>/<Control-Left> on the build in wt-a1.

    Before: R set |::var1 |value!!|!!!|, L |set |::|var1 |value!|!!|!!; R x |:= |y->|z|, L |x :|= |y-|>z.

    After: R set |::var1 |value!!!!!|, L |set |::|var1 |value|!!!!!; R x |:= |y->z|, L |x |:= y|->z.

    The second ticket line ("$value + $value2") is unchanged and was already coherent.

    • Test cluster-9.0/9.1/9.2 (startOfNextWord / endOfWord / startOfPreviousWord at every index of ab!!!cd ::ef, constraint needsICU) (fails before: yes, all 3; passes after: yes)
    • Test files run cluster 76/0, text 703/0, textIndex 190/0, entry 306/0, spinbox 324/0 with fix; main: cluster 76/3 (the new tests), others the same as with the fix
    • Notes:
      • A separate bug already exists in 8.6 and main and is not addressed here: TextPrevPos calls startOfPreviousWord $text end, and end is the last character, not the cursor position. Ctrl+Left therefore skips a one-character word right before the cursor ("a b c": from the end it goes to b, in 8.6.18 too; + $|value2 misses the stop before $).
      • endOfWord still does one next() when the boundary is followed by non-space, so endOfWord("ab!!!cd",0) is 5 (was 3). Ctrl+Right (endOfWord + startOfNextWord, from b4a14597) thus still stops only after whitespace, now consistently.
    • Windows Windows loads icu.dll/icuuc.dll (unversioned symbols). Check that ubrk_getRuleStatus resolves there (it is exported by the system icu.dll), then run cluster.test (needsICU must be true) and text.test/entry.test, and try the ticket snippet with Ctrl+Left/Right.
    • Suggested ticket comment Prototype in tkIcu.c: when moving by words, boundaries between two punctuation characters (ICU rule status UBRK_WORD_NONE) are skipped, so !!!!! or -> is one word for Ctrl+Left/Right, as in editors. With -locale tcl8 the 8.6 behaviour is still available.

    Open ticket b54adf4796 · 07. [entry] · filed 2024-01-19

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 9.0, 8.6, 8.6.8, 8.5)

    • Platform generic
    • Tested a minimal script with focus .e1; focus .e2 (comV1 calls update; comV2 sets ::v1) leaves .e1 state = invalid on all versions listed
    • Cause generic/ttk/ttkEntry.c EntryValidateChange returns TCL_BREAK whenever the value changed during validation (VALIDATION_SET_VALUE), even when it is only revalidating (focusin, focusout, validate), where there is no edit to reject. No test added, because it needs a WM that refuses the resize.)
    • Suggested ticket comment Reproduced on X11 with openbox, icewm and fvwm. When the WM keeps the wrapper size fixed, UpdateGeometryInfo sees the wrapper already has the requested size and returns without laying out the toplevel under the (removed) menubar. Prototype fix re-lays out the wrapper in that case.

    ⬜b8fcb265292023some inconsistencies in text entry widgets and textpatchREPRODUCED (main, 8.6): partly by design, partly a real bug

    Open ticket b8fcb26529 · 07. [entry] · filed 2023-09-12

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6): partly by design, partly a real bug

    • Platform generic (bindings and docs)
    • Tested main tip and 8.6 tip on Xvfb, using event generate with the ticket's 5 examples plus BackSpace and Control-h.
      • Typed characters: entry and text replace the selection only when the insert cursor is inside or adjacent to it. ttk::entry always replaces it. This is intentional per the code comments, but the man pages don't say so.
      • BackSpace: entry and ttk::entry delete any selection (ttk also moves the cursor to sel.first). text deletes the selection only when the cursor is inside or adjacent to it, which text.n contradicts.
      • A real bug: Text <Control-h> ignores the selection completely and deletes insert-1c. Starting from "abc def ghi" with sel 1.0-1.3 and the cursor at 1.3, you get "ab def ghi". It also doesn't respect grapheme clusters, although text.n says Control-h behaves like Backspace.
    • Cause library/text.tcl, the Text <Control-h> binding is a separate simplistic script and not the BackSpace logic. Docs: doc/text.n, doc/entry.n and doc/ttk_entry.n describe insertion and deletion imprecisely.
    • Prototype b8fcb26529.patch (46 changed lines, test text-39.1). Control-h now does the same as BackSpace (when !tk_strictMotif). text.n/entry.n now say that typed text replaces the selection only when the cursor is inside or adjacent to it. ttk_entry.n now says that typed text always replaces the selection. text.n's Backspace/Control-h item gets the same inside-or-adjacent condition. text.test and entry.test pass.
    • Suggested ticket comment The differences in typed-text replacement are intentional; the man pages now describe them. Text <Control-h> really was inconsistent (it ignored the selection and grapheme clusters) and now matches BackSpace; test text-39.1.

    • Platform X11
    • Tested counted blocking X round trips (poll calls, strace) for the ticket's 20x20 ttk::button grid on Xvfb. 8.6.8: 1716, 8.6.9: 1864, 8.6.13: 2653, 8.6 tip: 2597, 8.7: 2586, 9.0: 2628, main --disable-bidi: 2528, installed main: 1735. 8.6.10 is not installed. Tk_DeleteErrorHandler (generic/tkError.c) calls XSync on every 10th deletion when requests are outstanding (added 2018 in 3233521135).     • Prototype 290ce5f460.patch (tkError.c, +6/-2). The fix is to skip that XSync when no request was sent while the handler was active.

    • Patch changes the Entry and Spinbox <<Paste>> bindings now read the CLIPBOARD first (if {![catch {::tk::GetSelection %W CLIPBOARD} tk::Priv(data)]}, the same pattern as <<Cut>>/<<Copy>>) and only then delete the selection. Without this, now that X11 also deletes, pasting from an empty clipboard deleted the selection and inserted nothing. Checked: an empty clipboard now leaves "aaa bbb ccc". tk_textPaste already reads the clipboard first. Remaining (unchanged) design point: like the existing Windows/macOS behaviour, the selection is deleted even when the insert cursor is elsewhere, and the text goes to the cursor (ttk::entry moves the cursor to sel.first first). Original kept as 3c4435c9ae.patch.1024.
    Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Windows VM, generic patches
    • 3c4435c9ae: OK. entry-25.4 passes before and after on Windows (Windows already deleted the selection on paste). Repro: paste replaces the selection in entry/spinbox/text before and after. With an empty clipboard, entry and spinbox used to delete the selection (aaa ccc); now they keep it (aaa bbb ccc). text kept it either way. entry 307/0, spinbox 324/0.

    ⬜1bddba50f82022<Double-1> delivery difference between linux and windowspatchREPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    Open ticket 1bddba50f8 · 69. Events · filed 2022-04-21

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    • Platform Windows (also Aqua)
    • Tested not reproducible on Linux/X11 by design (X server timestamps); Windows repro script win/1bddba50f8.tcl (manual double-click, prints VERDICT)
    • Cause win/tkWinX.c → TkWinPointerEvent → generic/tkPointer.c:InitializeEvent sets ButtonPress time = TkGetMS() (GetTickCount at *processing* time), not the time the click happened. When a <<ListboxSelect>> handler blocks 1.5 s, the queued second click gets a timestamp ~1.5 s later than the first, so it fails the double-click interval check in tkBind.c. X11 uses server timestamps, so the double click survives. macOS has the same pattern (TkGetMS in tkPointer), matching fvogel's observation.
    • Prototype none (needs Windows to verify; fix = pass the native message time — GetMessageTime() on Windows, [NSEvent timestamp] on macOS — down to InitializeEvent, e.g. via a platform hook/static "current pointer event time" set by TkWinPointerEvent before Tk_UpdatePointer)
    • Suggested ticket comment On Windows/macOS, button events are timestamped when Tk processes them (TkGetMS in tkPointer.c InitializeEvent) instead of when the click occurred, so a blocking handler makes the second click look too late for a double click. Using GetMessageTime() for pointer events should make Windows behave like X11.
    Windows VM, run 2
    • Platform Windows
    • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)
    • Tested win/1bddba50f8.tcl, driven by work-win/w1bdd.tcl plus dblclick.ps1 (SetCursorPos + mouse_event, a real double click 100 ms apart in the interactive session).

    9.1: ListboxSelect=2 Double-1=0, press times differ by 1516 ms. 8.6: same, 1547 ms.

    With the prototype: ListboxSelect=1 Double-1=1. Control run: clicks 700 ms apart (Windows double-click time is 500) are correctly not a double click. bind, event, listbox, entry, text, button, scale, scrollbar and winfo tests: 2841 tests, 0 failed.

    • Cause generic/tkPointer.c:InitializeEvent stamps pointer events with TkGetMS() (GetTickCount) at processing time. After the handler blocks for 1.5 s, the queued second click looks 1.5 s late. win/tkWinX.c:GenerateXEvent does the same for key and wheel events.
    • Prototype 1bddba50f8.patch (10 changed lines): uses (Time)(DWORD)GetMessageTime() in InitializeEvent (under #ifdef _WIN32; tkPointer.c is only built on Windows and macOS) and in GenerateXEvent. There is no automated test, because it needs real queued input messages.

    Caveat: for Tk_UpdatePointer calls that do not come from a message (the mouse timer, warp), GetMessageTime() returns the time of the last retrieved message. That time is slightly older, which is harmless for crossing events. macOS needs the equivalent ([NSEvent timestamp]); not done.

    • Suggested ticket comment Confirmed on Windows (9.1, 8.6): button events are timestamped when Tk processes them (TkGetMS in tkPointer.c), not when the click happened. A patch that uses GetMessageTime() for pointer and key events on Windows is ready; with it the double click survives a blocking handler.
    Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Windows VM

    Open ticket 9fd94c63b0 · 05. Canvas Items · filed 2021-12-29

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6 on X11)

    • Platform generic
    • Tested X11 main, 8.6 with Liberation Serif Italic 200: text item bbox x=161..240 but glyph ink extends to x=270; after itemconfigure -fill red 561 black pixels remain at x=240..270.
    • Cause generic/tkCanvText.c:ComputeTextBbox uses the text layout's advance width, which excludes glyph overhang (right/left side bearing); the redraw area (item bbox) therefore misses the overhanging ink. Same limitation exists on all platforms that report advance widths.
    • Prototype none (a proper fix needs ink extents from the font backend, e.g. XftTextExtents x/width, plus separating the redraw region from the reported bbox, which many tests depend on; not small)
    • Suggested ticket comment Reproduced on X11 with an italic font. The canvas redraws only the item bbox, computed from advance widths, so glyph overhang is not refreshed. Fixing it needs ink-extent metrics from the font code; workaround: append a thin space (U+2009/U+202F).
    2026-10-25 prototype, Linux
    • Status PROTOTYPED
    • Cause generic/tkCanvText.c:ComputeTextBbox builds the item bbox from the layout's advance width, so ink that sticks out (italic or overhanging glyphs) lies outside it. tkCanvas.c uses that bbox for the damage area (EventuallyRedrawItem and the "old area" redraws in dchars/insert/rchars/imove), and DisplayCanvas uses it to choose which items to redraw. So the overhang is (a) never erased on recolor/move/delete, and (b) clipped away when the item is first drawn after the window is mapped. Point (b) was not in the ticket.
    • Fix new MODULE_SCOPE TkCanvTextRedrawMargin() in tkCanvText.c returns (ascent+descent)/4 for a visible text item. New static GetItemRedrawArea() in tkCanvas.c returns the item bbox grown by that margin on all four sides (this also covers -angle). It is used in EventuallyRedrawItem, in the 4 old-area sites and in the DisplayCanvas item-selection loop. bbox, find and the item header are unchanged. Measured overhang at -100px is at most 17px (DejaVu/Noto/Liberation/Free italic); the margin there is 25-34px. Not changed: the image subcommand (DrawCanvas) still selects items by bbox. 9fd94c63b0.patch (162 changed lines: about 60 are function header comments, 20 are the test, and the EventuallyRedrawItem change is mostly renaming itemPtr->x1 to local x1. That is over the 60-line guideline. It could be made smaller, e.g. by inlining the margin in EventuallyRedrawItem only, but then the display loop and the old-area sites stay wrong.)
    • Repro ticket script with Liberation Serif italic -150, recolor black to red. Before: 150 black pixels remain at x=222..237 (bbox x2=222). After: 0.
    • Test canvText-23.1 "glyph overhang is redrawn". It uses {Times -100 italic} "f", creates the item before the first update so the overhang is drawn, deletes the item, and counts non-white pixels in bbox±30 with testpixel (constraint testpixel). Fails before: yes (main: non-zero). Passes after: yes. With fontconfig all of Times/Helvetica/DejaVu/Liberation italic "f" overhang.     • Tested no live run, because the scenario needs a second X server besides my assigned display.     ⬜4476fd61442020X11 clip region ignored for Xft text drawingpatchREPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    Open ticket 4476fd6144 · 46. Unix Fonts · filed 2020-08-22

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    • Platform X11
    • Tested main (9.1b1, bidi/HarfBuzz backend) and 8.6 tip, Xvfb, no WM. A small stubs extension (work-a2/clip/clip.c) sets XSetClipRectangles(gc, 0,0,30x40) and calls Tk_DrawChars. Text is drawn out to x=252, so the 30px clip is ignored on both versions.
    • Cause unix/tkUnixRFont.c (and tkUnixBidiFont.c) only call XftDrawSetClip() from the thread-global tsdPtr->clipRegion, which is set by the private TkUnixSetXftClipRegion() (used only by ttkLabel/ttkEntry). The GC clip mask is never used, and Xlib gives no way to read it back from a GC. Also, Tk_ClipDrawableToRect (stub 284) is NULL/no-op on X11 and Windows.
    • Prototype none (needs a public API, i.e. a TIP-level change). Options: implement Tk_ClipDrawableToRect on X11 so it sets/clears the Xft clip region (this fills the NULL stub slot and removes the #undef/#define ... 0 in tkDecls.h/tkStubInit.c for non-Mac), or export TkUnixSetXftClipRegion through the platform stubs.
    • Suggested ticket comment Still present in 8.6 and main: Xft text drawing ignores the clip mask of the GC, and Xlib cannot read that clip mask back. A fix needs a public API. The candidate is Tk_ClipDrawableToRect(), which is a no-op on X11 today and could set the Xft clip region.
    2026-10-25 prototype, Linux
    • Status PROTOTYPED (core change only; no test in the suite)
    • Cause unix/tkUnixRFont.c and tkUnixBidiFont.c apply only the thread-global Xft clip set by the private TkUnixSetXftClipRegion() (used by ttkLabel/ttkEntry). The GC clip mask cannot be read back from Xlib. The public Tk_ClipDrawableToRect (stub 284) is NULL on X11: tkStubInit.c has #define Tk_ClipDrawableToRect 0 for every non-Mac build, and tkDecls.h has #undef for non-Mac. A stubs extension that calls it on main segfaults (verified).
    • Fix implement Tk_ClipDrawableToRect in unix/tkUnixDraw.c (X11 only). It keeps a per-thread Region, sets it with TkUnixSetXftClipRegion() under HAVE_XFT || HAVE_BIDI, and clears and frees it when width or height is negative. Without Xft it is a no-op, because core X fonts already honour the GC clip. The drawable argument is ignored: the clip applies to all Xft text drawing in the thread until reset. Only Xft text is clipped, not other Xlib drawing; the docs should say so (doc/3DBorder.3 is not updated yet). Stub table: no regeneration. tk.decls and the generated part of tkDecls.h are unchanged. In tkStubInit.c #else #define ... 0 became #elif defined(_WIN32). In the hand-written tail of tkDecls.h (below !END!) #ifndef MAC_OSX_TK became #ifdef _WIN32. So the slot is filled on X11, and Windows keeps the NULL slot. 4476fd6144.patch (63 changed lines, about 25 of them comments).
    • Repro work-a2/clip/clip2.c and t2.tcl (stubs extension: Tk_ClipDrawableToRect(0,0,30,40), Tk_DrawChars "WWWWWWWW", reset, draw another W). Main (bidi): SIGSEGV through the NULL stub. Patched, both --disable-bidi (Xft/RFont) and --enable-bidi (HarfBuzz) builds: ink stops at x=29 (unclipped: 252), and text drawn after the reset is not clipped.
    • Test none. Tk_ClipDrawableToRect cannot be called from Tcl. A test would need a tkTest.c helper (e.g. testfont drawclipped font window text x y w h, ~30 lines) plus a testpixel check. Left out to keep the change under ~80 lines.
    • Test files run (with fix, --disable-bidi): font 304/0, unixFont 50/0, canvText 100/0, textDisp 409/0, frame 207/0, entry 306/0, ttk entry 44/0. The fix only adds a function, so main results are the same. The bidi build compiles without warnings.
    • Windows nothing changes there (slot stays NULL). Separately, decide whether Windows should also implement it, e.g. with SelectClipRgn.
    • Suggested ticket comment Prototype: implement Tk_ClipDrawableToRect() on X11 (stub slot 284 was NULL there) so that it sets the Xft clip region, as TkUnixSetXftClipRegion() does for ttk. Extensions like Tktable can then call it around Tk_DrawChars()/Tk_DrawTextLayout(). On X11 it affects only Xft text; other drawing still uses the GC clip.

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6), but it is not what the ticket assumes. The forms are documented in GetCursor.3 (and options.n refers to it). On X11 {name fg bg} colors are ignored because libXcursor replaces a glyph cursor from the cursor font with the themed ARGB cursor whenever source font == mask font and mask = source+1, and it ignores the colors (with XCURSOR_CORE=1 the colors work). {name fg} and @src fg / @src mask fg bg do work. @src alone is documented as Windows-only (the ticket's failing .xbm test). There is also a doc typo in GetCursor.3: "standard X cursor cursor", "cursorcursor.h" (should be cursor font / cursorfont.h).

    • Cause unix/tkUnixCursor.c:CreateCursorFromTableOrFile() calls XCreateGlyphCursor(cursorFont, cursorFont, shape, shape+1, ...), and XcursorTryShapeCursor() replaces that call.
    • Prototype e1d86b048b.patch (72 lines). When colors other than black/white are given, it loads a second font ID for the mask so that Xcursor does not replace the cursor. It also fixes the GetCursor.3 typo and documents the theme behaviour. {watch red blue} now shows red/blue, while watch and {watch black white} keep the themed cursor. cursor.test passes. No automated test (it would need XFixes).
    • Suggested ticket comment On X11 the Xcursor library replaces cursor-font cursors with themed ones and ignores the colors given as {name fg bg}. The branch uses a separate mask font ID when colors are given, so they are honoured again. The @file forms work as documented (@file alone is Windows-only).

    • Cause unix/Makefile.in: objects have no header dependencies.
    • Prototype a0109510a8.patch (6 changed lines, unix only). It adds a coarse rule $(OBJS) $(WISH_OBJS) $(TKTEST_OBJS) $(STUB_LIB_OBJS): $(DEP_HDRS), where DEP_HDRS is generic/*.h, ttk/*.h and unix/*.h (X11) or macosx/*.h + xlib (Aqua). Any header change rebuilds all objects. The rule sits after all:, so the default goal is unchanged. A finer alternative is -MMD -MP with -include *.d (gcc/clang only). win/Makefile.in would need the same rule.
    • Suggested ticket comment Still true on main. A coarse fix is to make all objects depend on the source headers (prototype: rebuild everything when any .h changes). Per-file dependencies would need -MMD (gcc/clang).

    Open ticket a34b49f8c6 · 18. [text] · filed 2017-06-25

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6) for part (b), the "arrows" drawn with tags that combine -tabs and -justify right. Fixed on revised_text. Parts (a) and (c) were script errors or are fixed on revised_text. On revised_text, textDisp-2.26 and textDisp-24.24 are still marked knownBug and still fail if forced (2.26: 48 78 108 138; 24.24: {14 7 7 13} {28 20 7 13}), because the branch intentionally applies tabs under right/center justify.

    • Platform X11 (the drawing part); generic (the revised_text test part)
    • Tested main 9.1 and 8.6 tip (the bug shows: HT glyphs are drawn and "d e" are pushed out of the window), revised_text tip (correct tab layout). All on Xvfb, no WM, Liberation Mono.
    • Cause generic/tkTextDisp.c. For -justify right/center, LayoutDLine does not split chunks at tabs, and MeasureChars gives an embedded tab zero width. CharDisplayProc strips only a trailing tab, so embedded tabs are passed to Tk_DrawCharsInContext. Xft draws them as the U+2409-like control glyph and measures that glyph into the prefix width, so every later character is shifted right and clipped. Windows (the #else branch) uses the same path, but GDI draws nothing for a tab.
    • Prototype a34b49f8c6.patch (tkTextDisp.c, +45/-23, only the TK_DRAW_IN_CONTEXT branch of CharDisplayProc). It draws each run between tabs separately, positioned from the layout width (tabs count as 0) and in the context of the text since the previous tab; underline and overstrike are handled the same way. With no tabs it makes exactly the same call as before. After the fix the window shows "abcde" right-aligned and underlined; textDisp, text and textTag tests pass. No automated test, because only rendering changes and bbox output was already correct. It is independent of the a0c1a9c6c6 prototype, which touches other hunks of the same file.
    • Suggested ticket comment On main/8.6 X11, a tag with -tabs and -justify right/center gives embedded tabs zero width in the layout, but CharDisplayProc still passes them to Xft, which draws a control glyph and shifts the rest of the line out of view. Fix prototyped: draw only the runs between tabs (branch TBD). The revised_text part is fixed there.

On main, unix/tkUnixInt.h defines TK_DRAW_IN_CONTEXT only #ifdef HAVE_BIDI, so a --disable-bidi build (and every non-bidi build) still drew the HT glyphs. I added the same run-between-tabs drawing to the #else (Tk_DrawChars) branch. VERIFIED in both builds.

    ⬜5d5c43acad2016linux: when window is maximized, menu replacement failspatchREPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    • Platform X11
    • Tested main and 8.6 under openbox, icewm and fvwm (bug) and under twm and no WM (no maximize, OK). After . configure -menu .n in a maximized window, the new menubar is 1 px high and the toplevel is placed at y=1 in the wrapper.

Verified under openbox, icewm and fvwm. unixWm, wm, menu and unixMenu tests show the same results as before, with and without openbox.
    • Suggested ticket comment Still present in 8.6/9.x. UpdateGeometryInfo() skips re-laying out the wrapper when the maximized window's size doesn't change, so the new menubar keeps its initial 1-pixel height. Prototype fix available.

    ⬜6e3c4fb23f2015Can crash on Tk load in separate thread after Tk loaded in main threadREPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    Open ticket 6e3c4fb23f · 99. Other · filed 2015-03-10

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    • Platform X11
    • Tested a C driver (work-a2/thr.c: every thread does CreateInterp, Tk_Init, a canvas with text items, then destroy and Tcl_FinalizeThread). It was linked against main and 8.6 tip and run on Xvfb.
      • With Xft fonts, both versions fail after 1–3 rounds, even with a single worker thread at a time and whether or not Tk is also loaded in the main thread. The X error is BadRequest/BadLength ("major opcode 0") in XRenderQueryFormats ← XftDefaultSubstitute ← tkUnixRFont.c:InitFont. Calling XInitThreads() first does not help.
      • Without fonts (frame only), 8.6 passes serially and with 4–8 concurrent threads.
      • Without fonts on main, serial runs pass, but concurrent threads abort with a glib assertion or malloc corruption in tk_atk_accessible_finalize.
    • Cause two separate problems.

    1. The per-display cache in libXrender/libXft goes stale once one thread's display has been closed with XCloseDisplay (TkpCloseDisplay at thread exit) and another thread opens a new one. Tk's own font code has only thread-specific data.

    2. main only (a new regression): unix/tkUnixAccessibility.c keeps process-global unlocked state (tk_to_atk_map, toplevel_accessible_objects, tk_root_accessible, acc_context), so ATK is not thread-safe.

    • Prototype none. (1) is outside Tk or would need a redesign, for example never closing displays or sharing one per process. (2) needs a mutex or thread-specific data across the ATK module and should be its own ticket.
    • Suggested ticket comment Still reproducible on 8.6 and main on Linux. Creating Tk in successive threads fails in Xft/Xrender (XRenderQueryFormats) after a previous thread closed its display, even without concurrency. On main, the new ATK accessibility code also has unprotected global state that crashes when Tk runs concurrently in several threads.
    ⬜0cb85bcb482014Mysteries when all default named fonts are deletedREPRODUCED (main, 8.6); this is a design question more than a bug

    Open ticket 0cb85bcb48 · 44. Generic Fonts · filed 2014-10-24

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6); this is a design question more than a bug

    • Platform generic
    • Tested main, 8.6 on Xvfb, no WM
    • Cause font delete on a named font that is still in use only marks it deletePending. Widgets that hold it (and new widgets that ask for the same name while it is held) keep the old attributes. After the last reference goes away, "TkFixedFont" etc. are parsed as a font description with an unknown family. They then fall back to the system default (here Noto Sans 12, proportional), so the text widget metrics change. Recreating the named font updates all users. font configure TkFixedFont errors even while the font is still effectively in use.
    • Prototype none (policy decision: whether to make the Tk* standard fonts undeletable, or reset them instead of deleting)
    • Suggested ticket comment Still the same on main and 8.6. Deleted standard fonts silently fall back to the default font (proportional) once no widget holds them. Should font delete refuse, or reset instead, for the Tk* standard fonts?

    ⬜3598564fff2012wm maxsize does not take into account task bar on Windows 7REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    Open ticket 3598564fff · 62. Geometry Functions · filed 2012-12-26

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    • Platform Windows
    • Tested code reading; Windows repro: win/3598564fff.tcl
    • Cause win/tkWinWm.c UpdateGeometryInfo/GetMaxSize. defMaxWidth/Height come from WM_GETMINMAXINFO ptMaxTrackSize, which is the whole screen and not the work area, so the natural size is clamped only to the screen height. A fix would clamp the default (not the user-set) maxsize to the monitor's rcWork (GetMonitorInfo). That changes behaviour and could also restrict manual resizing, so it needs discussion.
    • Prototype none (behaviour change; untestable here)
    • Suggested ticket comment The default maxsize comes from ptMaxTrackSize, which covers the whole screen and does not exclude the task bar. Clamping the default maximum to the monitor work area (rcWork) would fix this. X11 behaves the same way with panels.
    Windows VM, run 3

    • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    ⬜3466588fff2011panedwindow resize problem with text -setgrid 1patchREPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    Open ticket 3466588fff · 48. Geometry Management · filed 2011-12-28

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

    • Platform Windows
    • Tested main, 8.6.18, 8.5.19 on X11: no WM, openbox, icewm. The toplevel's pixel size never changes when the sash is moved. The gridded size reported by wm geometry does change (64x30 -> 64x24).
    • Cause the panedwindow's requested height depends on the sash position, so the grid base size (reqHeight - gridHeight*inc) changes when the sash moves. On X11 the pixel size is kept, so only the reported grid units change. tkWinWm apparently keeps the grid units instead, so the pixel size changes. The fix would be in tkWinWm.c.
    • Prototype none (Windows only). Repro: win/3466588fff.tcl
    • Suggested ticket comment Not reproducible on X11: the toplevel keeps its pixel size, and only the grid units reported by wm geometry change. Probably a tkWinWm.c issue.
    Windows VM, run 4
    • Platform Windows
    • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)
    • Tested win/3466588fff.tcl on both. Each sash move makes the toplevel grow: 518x490 → 586 → 682 → 746 px high (the last move is -40 px, and the growth stops at the screen size). wm geometry stays 64x30 and then becomes 64x28.
    • Cause not pinned down. After a sash move the panedwindow requests its current pane sizes, so the grid base (reqHeight - reqGridHeight*inc) grows. win/tkWinWm.c UpdateGeometryInfo keeps wmPtr->height in grid units (30), so the pixel height grows by (30-24)*16 px each time. X11 converts back from the actual pixel size and does not grow. The tkUnixWm.c and tkWinWm.c formulas are the same; the difference is probably in the ConfigureNotify/ConfigureTopLevel feedback.
    • Prototype none (needs more study of the Windows grid feedback)
    • Suggested ticket comment Still present in 8.6.18 and 9.1 on Windows 11: every sash move grows the gridded toplevel by (gridHeight - text -height) lines. X11 is not affected.
    2026-10-25 prototype, Windows VM
    • Status PROTOTYPED
    • Cause win/tkWinWm.c:TopLevelReqProc schedules UpdateGeometryInfo for every geometry request. After a sash move, the panedwindow requests its current pane sizes, so the toplevel's reqHeight grows. With a gridded explicit size (wmPtr->height = 30, reqGridHeight 24), UpdateGeometryInfo then computes reqHeight + 6*inc and the window grows. unix/tkUnixWm.c:TopLevelReqProc ignores geometry requests when explicit dimensions are set, so on X11 the pixel size stays and wm geometry reports 64x24. The triage guess about ConfigureTopLevel was wrong: both platforms convert the size back in the same way.
    • Fix TopLevelReqProc returns early when wmPtr->width >= 0 && wmPtr->height >= 0, as on X11. The TK_GEOMETRYREQ message for embedded windows is still sent first. For non-gridded windows nothing changes, because the size then does not depend on the request. 3466588fff.patch (32 changed lines: 8 tkWinWm.c, 24 test)
    • Repro work-win/p1025/pw.tcl. Before: 518x490 becomes 518x586 after the first sash move, wm geometry stays 64x30. After: 518x490 throughout, wm geometry 64x24, identical to X11 (Xvfb, no WM: 520x522, 64x24).
    • Test panedwindow-31.2 "sash move does not resize a gridded toplevel" (fails before: yes, on Windows; passes after: yes; passes on X11 as before)
    • Test files run panedwindow 413/0, winWm 37/0, wm 302/0, grid 206/0, pack 199/0, place 56/0, geometry 14/0, text 703/0, frame 207/0 with fix; main panedwindow 412/0, the others identical
    • Windows done. Worth checking by hand: interactive resizing of a gridded toplevel, and menubar add/remove on a window with an explicit size (TopLevelReqProc no longer triggers UpdateGeometryInfo there, but menubar changes call it directly).
    • Suggested ticket comment On Windows every geometry request recomputes a gridded toplevel's size from its new requested size, and the panedwindow's request grows after a sash move. X11 ignores geometry requests once the size is set explicitly. A prototype does the same in tkWinWm.c (test panedwindow-31.2), and the toplevel now keeps its size as on X11.

    • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)
    • Tested 3009885_auto.tcl (automatic). The message box, with parent a transient of ., returns {} right after wm deiconify . (3528 ms), with no click. wm frame .t changes (0xd50102 → 0xaf1b32), so the transient's wrapper was recreated.
    • Cause win/tkWinWm.c WmWaitVisibilityOrMapProc calls UpdateWrapper(transient) on every VisibilityNotify of the container. That destroys the transient's wrapper HWND, and with it the MessageBox it owns, so MessageBoxW returns 0 and the result is "".

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6, 8.5)

    • Platform X11
    • Tested script "menu + label; fullscreen 1; -menu {}; -menu .menu; fullscreen 0" on Xvfb with no WM, openbox, fvwm, icewm. No WM: OK. With update between steps: icewm leaves the window at 1280x955 (main)/1280x961 (8.6) instead of 40x24; openbox/fvwm OK.
