Fullscreen-only or menu-only toggles are fine.
    • Cause unix/tkUnixWm.c. While fullscreen, ConfigureEvent treats the WM's fullscreen resize as a user resize and stores it in wmPtr->width/height; a menubar change then makes UpdateGeometryInfo XResizeWindow the wrapper to that size (minus/plus the menu height), and some WMs (icewm) record this as the size to restore. In the no-update race, a resize issued with the not-yet-sized new menubar clone (menuHeight 1) is applied by the WM after unfullscreen and taken as user size (height = 25 - 35 = -10).
    • Prototype 2865578fff.patch (104 lines diff, ~45 changed: factor the toplevel/menubar layout tail of ConfigureEvent into LayoutWrapper(); UpdateGeometryInfo only calls LayoutWrapper while attributes.fullscreen; ConfigureEvent does not record user size while reqState/attributes.fullscreen). Fixes the realistic case (steps separated by event processing) on icewm/fvwm/openbox and keeps natural size tracking after a fullscreen round trip; does NOT fix the all-in-one-idle race of sample.tk on fvwm/icewm. No new test (WM-dependent); wm.test, unixWm.test, menu.test, unixMenu.test pass under fvwm.
    • Suggested ticket comment Still reproducible on X11 (icewm, fvwm, openbox) with 8.6 and 9.1. While fullscreen, Tk records the fullscreen size as a user-requested size and resizes the wrapper on menubar changes; icewm then restores to that size. A prototype that leaves the wrapper size alone while fullscreen fixes the case with event processing between steps.
    Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Linux
    • Status VERIFIED
    • Applies to main clean (offsets 8-11 lines)
    • Repro work-a2/2865578.tcl (menu+label; fullscreen 1; -menu {}; -menu .menu; fullscreen 0, with update/pause between the steps). icewm before: 37x24 → 1280x954 (BAD, 2 of 2 runs).     • Patch changes none. Review: while fullscreen, the early return in UpdateGeometryInfo leaves WM_MOVE_PENDING set, so a pending move is applied after leaving fullscreen. That is acceptable. As noted at triage, the all-in-one-idle race in the ticket's sample is not fixed.

    • Tested main, 8.6 on X11 (Courier, Arial, Helvetica). After each font shrink step, the screen grab equals the grab after a forced full redraw, so no traces are left.
    • Cause per dkf, the bbox of rotated text (ComputeTextBbox/TkIntersectAngledTextLayout) disagrees with GDI glyph placement, which rounds cumulatively. Windows-specific.
    • Prototype none. Repro: win/2816150fff.tcl (compares PowerShell screen grabs before and after a forced full redraw, prints BUG PRESENT/NOT PRESENT)
    • Suggested ticket comment Not reproducible on X11 (main, 8.6). Needs a check on current Windows with win/2816150fff.tcl-style repro (Arial is the font reported to show it).
    Windows VM, run 5
    • Platform Windows
    • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)
    • Tested win/2816150fff.tcl, capturing with act.exe (deterministic; a repeated capture shows no noise). 8.6: Arial 28→22 leaves a few pixels of the previous size's last "t" beyond the new bbox (3–16 px per step). 9.1: Arial is clean in this run, but "Courier" (a raster font that GDI cannot rotate, so it is drawn unrotated) leaves traces at 6 sizes, because the bbox is computed for rotated text.
    • Cause ComputeTextBbox (generic/tkCanvText.c) computes the bbox from Tk's unrotated metrics. GDI places the rotated glyphs with its own cumulative rounding, and does not rotate raster fonts at all, so the drawn ink can fall outside the bbox.
    • Prototype none. Padding the bbox of angled text by a few pixels would hide the Arial case. The raster-font case needs a check in win/tkWinFont.c that the angled font really rotates.

    2026-10-25 prototype, Windows VM
    • Status PROTOTYPED
    • Cause win/tkWinFont.c:MultiFontTextOut draws angled text with GetScreenFont(angle). GDI ignores lfEscapement for raster fonts ("Courier" = coure.fon on 9.x, Fixedsys, Terminal), so their text is drawn horizontally while the item's bbox (generic/tkCanvText.c:ComputeTextBbox) is rotated. The ink falls outside the bbox, so when the font shrinks or the item changes it is not erased. For TrueType fonts the problem is now small: a probe of Arial, Courier New and Times New Roman at sizes 9–31 and angles 15/30/90/200 found ink outside the bbox only once (2 px, Times New Roman 9 at 90°). Arial is clean in 9.1.
    • Fix a new helper DrawUnrotatableRun() is called for angled runs. If the selected angled font is not a vector font (!(tmPitchAndFamily & TMPF_VECTOR)), it creates the same LOGFONT with OUT_TT_ONLY_PRECIS and the raster font's pitch and family (Courier gives Courier New), and draws the run's characters with ExtTextOutW. The lpDx widths are summed from the shaped glyph advances through logClust, so the text length matches Tk's measurement. RTL runs and TrueType fonts take the old path unchanged. Bbox padding is not included: 9fd94c63b0's redraw margin already covers the remaining 1–2 px TrueType rounding and the slightly wider substitute glyphs. The patch is independent of 3249f7d4c6.patch: both apply on top of each other in either order, since 3249 only changes FamilyExists/WinFontExistProc and winfont-2.8/13.1. 2816150fff.patch (101 changed lines: 69 in tkWinFont.c, of which about 25 are the function header and declaration, plus 32 test lines. That is over the 60-line guideline.)
    • Repro ink pixels outside the bbox (work-win/p1025/rot.tcl, "Angled text", sizes 9/14/22/31). Before: Courier 90° 193/189/171/153, Courier 200° 103/100/31, Fixedsys 90° 275/275/242/208, Fixedsys 15°/30°/200° 5–145. After: everything 0, except Courier 90° at sizes 9 and 14 (10 and 4 px), where the raster size snapping makes Tk's metrics differ from Courier New's. A screenshot shows correctly rotated Courier, Fixedsys and Terminal text inside its bbox, including at 200°. testwingdiobjects shows no GDI leak after 600 redraws.
    • Test winfont-6.2 "Tk_DrawAngledChars procedure: raster font is rotated" (Fixedsys -angle 90, full redraw, counts non-white pixels outside the bbox with testpixel) (fails before: yes, 51; passes after: yes)
    • Test files run winFont 57/0, canvText 100/0, font 304/0 with fix; main winFont 56/0, canvText 100/0, font 304/0
    • Windows done on Windows 11. Not checked: vertical (@-prefixed) fonts, and a raster subfont used as a fallback for missing characters.
    • Suggested ticket comment On Windows 11 the traces now come mainly from raster fonts (e.g. "Courier", Fixedsys): GDI cannot rotate them, so the text is drawn horizontally outside the rotated bbox. The prototype draws such runs with a rotated TrueType substitute, spaced like the original glyphs (test winfont-6.2). The 1–2 px TrueType rounding is covered by the redraw margin of 9fd94c63b0.

    Full verdict

    REGRESSED on 8.6 tip. dgp fixed it in 8.5.10 (Tcl_GetDouble replaced by sscanf("%lf")). Check-in e0dd3fdb59 (2026-09-22, core-8-6-branch, "Fix 9e89324a0a: spinbox parsing depends on the LC_NUMERIC locale") switched back to Tcl_GetDouble, so the octal problem is back on 8.6: with -format %02.0f the value goes 07 → 08 → 00. It is also silently wrong: "010" is read as 8. main and 9.x are not affected because Tcl 9 has no octal parsing.

    • Platform generic
    • Tested main 9.1 OK. 8.6 tip (8.6.18) FAIL: 06 07 08 00 01 02 11 10 09 00 00. 8.6.15, 8.6.13, 8.6.8, 8.5.19 and 8.4 OK.

It adds a helper GetSpinboxValue() that skips leading zeros after an optional sign and then calls Tcl_GetDouble, so the value is still parsed independently of the locale. Verified with 8.6 tip plus the patch: the repro is fixed, spinbox.test (293 passed) and entry.test pass, and the new test fails without the patch. It is only needed for 8.6 (harmless on 9.x).
    • Suggested ticket comment The fix for 9e89324a0a (e0dd3fdb59) reintroduced this on core-8-6-branch: Tcl_GetDouble("08") fails and "010" becomes 8 in Tcl 8.6. We propose stripping leading zeros before Tcl_GetDouble (8.6 only). Test spinbox-26.2.

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6, 8.5). This is intended behaviour, but it is not documented, so it is a DOCS issue.

    • Platform generic
    • Tested main, 8.6 tip, 8.5 on X11 (Xvfb, no WM), using event generate. A click focuses ttk::button/checkbutton with -takefocus 1 and does not with -takefocus 0. ttk::frame -takefocus 1 does not take focus on click. Classic buttons never take focus on click.
    • Cause library/ttk/button.tcl binds TButton <Button-1> to ttk::clickToFocus, which checks ttk::takesFocus, i.e. -takefocus (library/ttk/utils.tcl). TCheckbutton and TRadiobutton copy this binding.

    • Platform generic (X11 part tested; Windows part NEEDS-WINDOWS)
    • Tested main and 8.6 tip on X11, tk_getSaveFile filled in by script. With -defaultextension "" + -filetypes {{xyz *.xyz}} the result is myfile.xyz. With the option absent it is also myfile.xyz. With .abc it is myfile.abc.
    • Cause On Unix, tkfbox.tcl declares the option with default "", and SetFilter replaces an empty value with the filter's extension, so "" and absent cannot be told apart. On Windows (win/tkWinDialog.c), SetDefaultExtension() is called only when the option is given. IFileDialog treats an empty default extension as "use the selected filter's extension", so "" appends .xyz and absent appends nothing.
    • Prototype 2045553fff.patch (29 lines, docs only: getOpenFile.n describes the actual per-platform behaviour). The alternative is to make the behaviour uniform (danckaert, 2015): "" means no extension and absent means use the filter extension. That needs a "was specified" flag in tkfbox.tcl, and on Windows SetDefaultExtension(L"") when the option is absent but -filetypes is present. It is small (about 20 lines) but changes behaviour on Windows. Windows repro (manual input: type myfile twice): win/2045553fff.tcl
    • Suggested ticket comment Still present in 9.1/8.6. On X11, "" and an absent -defaultextension behave the same (the extension is taken from -filetypes). On Windows only "" adds it. Either the man page is fixed (prototype) or the behaviour is unified (""=none, absent=from filetype).
    Windows VM, run 3
    • Platform Windows
    • Verdict f436621c1a REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip), prototype verified. 2045553fff REPRODUCED ("" and absent differ); doc prototype verified and extended.
    • Tested dialogs driven with act.exe (filename set in Edit 1001, file type changed by a real click on the type combobox plus Down/Enter, then IDOK). Results:
      • ref91 and 8.6: no -defaultextension gives foo (case 1), and also foo after switching to Text (case 2). -defaultextension "" gives the type's extension (foo.tcl, or foo.txt with Text selected). 2045 script: "" gives myfile.xyz, absent gives myfile.
      • Additional check (work-win/dx.tcl, ref91): -defaultextension .dat with first type {"All files" *} gives foo.dat; with first type {Text .txt} gives foo.txt; with {"Tcl or Text" {.tcl .txt}} gives foo.tcl. On Windows the extension of the selected type wins over -defaultextension (on Unix an explicit -defaultextension wins, see tkfbox.tcl SetFilter).
      • Prototype: case 1 foo.tcl, case 2 foo.txt (follows the type selector). 2045 script: both give myfile.xyz. The dx.tcl results do not change.
      • New tests winDialog-5.7.9 (first type .txt gives bar.txt) and 5.7.10 (-typevariable picks .tcl) fail on ref91 and pass with the prototype. winDialog-5.7.* (47af) still pass.
    • Cause win/tkWinDialog.c GetFileNameVista calls SetDefaultExtension only when -defaultextension is given. With an empty default extension, IFileDialog uses the selected type's extension and follows type changes.
    • Prototype f436621c1a.patch (57 lines, reworked). When -defaultextension is absent for a save dialog, it now just calls SetDefaultExtension(L"") (7 code lines), replacing the earlier filter-parsing version. It includes getOpenFile.n text for the patched behaviour and the two tests. 2045553fff.patch (doc only, for unpatched behaviour) is updated with the rule that the selected type's extension wins on Windows. Use one of the two doc texts, depending on whether f436 goes in.
    • Suggested ticket comment f436621c1a: On Windows, IFileDialog uses the selected file type's extension only if some default extension is set. Setting an empty one when -defaultextension is not given makes Windows behave like Unix, including following the type selector. Patch and tests are ready. 2045553fff: confirmed that "" and an absent option differ on Windows; this goes away with the f436621c1a fix.

    ⬜1755313fff2007Big difference in speed between x and y scrolling in text wiREPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    Open ticket 1755313fff · 18. [text] · filed 2007-07-17

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    • Tested attached ttext3.tcl on Xvfb. main: X 0.13 s, Y 3.1 s. 8.6.18: X 0.04 s, Y 2.2 s. yview scroll 25 units takes about 300 ms per step, compared with 2.5 ms for xview scroll. Main is about 1.4x slower than 8.6 here.

    ⬜1660811fff2007Wrong rendering in transparent areaspatchREPRODUCED (main, 8.6, 8.4). This is by design: frames with -background {} are not transparent.

    Open ticket 1660811fff · 02. Appearance · filed 2007-02-15

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6, 8.4). This is by design: frames with -background {} are not transparent.

    • Platform X11 (generic)
    • Tested Xvfb, no WM, the reporter's script with a screen grab (PIL). After destroy .c.f2.f1, the padding area of .c.f2 (bg {}) keeps stale cyan pixels instead of the canvas red. The result is the same on main, 8.6 and 8.4.20.
    • Cause an empty background sets the X window background to None, and Tk never draws the frame. So the server never repaints the exposed areas. X11 has no true child-window transparency without compositing or the Shape extension (dkf's comment).
    • Prototype 1660811fff.patch (docs only, 40 lines). frame.n and toplevel.n now say that an empty -background does not make the widget transparent and that uncovered areas may show stale contents.
    • Suggested ticket comment Still the case on 8.6 and 9.x. An empty -background gives the window a None background, so Tk never repaints it. Real transparency is not possible there. Proposed documenting this in frame.n/toplevel.n and closing.

    ⬜1233834fff2005Restrictions on window resizing in Motif wmsREPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    Open ticket 1233834fff · 67. Unix Window Operations · filed 2005-07-06

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 8.6)

    • Platform X11
    • Tested my own repro, a mapped toplevel with wm resizable .t 0 0 then 1 1, after which the content grows to 480x500. Under mwm the window stays 200x150 on both main and 8.6 (2026-10-10). Without the toggle, mwm resizes it to 480x500. With no WM and with icewm, 480x500 is correct. twm needs interactive placement, so I could not test it.

    ⬜1191258fff2005text/entry become very inefficients with long linesREPRODUCED (main, 9.0, 8.6, 8.5; the slowdown is much milder than the "tens of seconds" reported in 2015)

    Open ticket 1191258fff · 18. [text] · filed 2005-04-27

    Full verdict

    REPRODUCED (main, 9.0, 8.6, 8.5; the slowdown is much milder than the "tens of seconds" reported in 2015)

    • Platform generic
    • Tested Xvfb, a single line of N×"x", -wrap char/word. N=100k: typing and cursor moves take 10–16 ms and see end 0.09 s (8.6) / 0.17 s (main), so it is usable. Scaling is quadratic: see end N=200k 0.39 s/0.45 s, N=400k 1.85 s/2.2 s (8.6 / main non-bidi build). The installed main build with bidi is about 2x slower (IsSimpleOnly in unix/tkUnixBidiFont.c rescans the rest of the string). 8.4.20 is about 2.5x faster, with the same growth.
    • Cause every display line is laid out with scans that run to the end of the logical-line segment. In generic/tkTextDisp.c, LayoutDLine's tab search (for (p = ... ; *p != 0; p++)) and MeasureChars's search for the next '\t'/'\n' are both O(rest of segment), and TkTextFindDisplayLineEnd/MeasureUp lay out from the start of the logical line. So one pass over a line of n chars costs O(n²/width).
    • Prototype none. The scans would have to be bounded (e.g. scan in windows while Tk_MeasureChars still fits) or the next-special-char position cached per segment. Doing that safely with TK_WHOLE_WORDS and the -wrap word logic is more than a small change.
    • Suggested ticket comment Still quadratic in the length of one logical line: LayoutDLine and MeasureChars in tkTextDisp.c scan to the end of the segment for every display line. It is usable at 100k chars (about 0.1–0.2 s for see end) but takes about 2 s at 400k.

    • 💬 2026-09-24 serhiy.storchaka: Fixed by 437a4bc8fb (lax UTF-8 parsing in Tk_MeasureChars): not reproducible with 8.6 and 9.0. The text widget still measures a partial UTF-8 character in CharBboxProc, which gives wrong results with the new bidi text rendering in 9.1: 7579c335f1.

    • Tested Greek text, comparing .t bbox width with font measure for each character: 12 of 17 wrong on main (bidi build), 0 elsewhere. No WM.
    • Cause generic/tkTextDisp.c:CharBboxProc measures the character at byteIndex as the byte range [byteIndex, byteIndex+1], which cuts a multi-byte UTF-8 character in half.       • 🔧 2026-09-28 check-in f27b1cfd17 on button-overrelief-after-click, in main, 9.0: Fix [1100518]: Button should stay in -overrelief state after event
      • 🔧 2026-09-29 check-in 3aa08341e5 on trunk, in main, 9.0, 8.6: Fix for 1100518fff: Button should stay in -overrelief state after event.

      • 🔧 2026-09-29 check-in ee0599a59d on core-8-6-branch, in main, 9.0, 8.6: Fix for 1100518fff: Button should stay in -overrelief state after event.
      • 💬 2026-09-28 serhiy.storchaka: Still reproducible with 9.1 on X11 and Windows. tk::ButtonUp restores the saved -relief after a click even if the mouse is still in the button, so -overrelief is lost until the mouse leaves and enters the button again. Checkbuttons and radiobuttons are not affected. Proposed fix in branch button-overrelief-after-click, with test button-16.1.
      • 💬 2026-09-29 nemethi: Serhiy, many thanks for fixing this very long-standing bug! The fix is now merged into main, core-9-0-branch, and core-8-6-branch by commits 3aa08341, 50f72925, and ee0599a5. Closing the ticket.
      Full verdict

      REPRODUCED (main, 8.6, 8.5, 8.4)

      • Platform generic (X11, Windows, Aqua all share the logic in library/button.tcl)
      • Tested event generate Enter/Press/Release/Leave on main, 8.6.18, 8.5.19 and 8.4.20 (Xvfb, no WM). All give enter=raised press=sunken release=flat when release=raised is expected.
      • Cause library/button.tcl, ::tk::ButtonUp (all three variants: x11, win32, aqua). It always restores the cached -relief (Priv($w,relief)) and forgets the cache, even when the pointer is still over the button (Priv(window) eq $w).
      • Prototype 1100518fff.patch (57+/6-, 132-line diff; most of it is the same 13-line change repeated in 3 procs). If the pointer is still inside and -overrelief is set, ButtonUp switches to the overrelief and keeps the cached relief so that ButtonLeave can restore it later.       ⬜1081603fff2004Arc item anomalies under WindowspatchREPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip) for the 1-px endpoint mismatch. The small-extent arcs are no longer full circles (fixed part confirmed).

      Open ticket 1081603fff · 05. Canvas Items · filed 2004-12-08

      Full verdict

      REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip) for the 1-px endpoint mismatch. The small-extent arcs are no longer full circles (fixed part confirmed).

      • Platform Windows
      • Tested not on X11 (the reporter and fvogel say X11 is correct). Repro script: win/1081603fff.tcl (visual yes/no check of the rounded square, fvogel's 0–90 pie, and the small arcs).
      • Cause win/tkWinDraw.c DrawOrFillArc computes the radial endpoints with integer rounding and passes them to GDI Arc/Pie/Chord. GDI's bounding-rectangle convention (right/bottom exclusive) and its endpoint-ray semantics differ from X11, so the endpoints can be off by a pixel.
      • Prototype none (needs Windows to verify pixel-exact output)
      • Suggested ticket comment The full-circle part is fixed via 6051a9fc. The remaining 1-px endpoint mismatch is still unverified; the repro script is in the triage set.
      Windows VM, run 5
      • Platform Windows
      • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip) for the 1-px endpoint mismatch. The small-extent arcs are no longer full circles (fixed part confirmed).
      • Tested arcs.tcl captured on Windows and on X11 (Xvfb, main). 9.1 and 8.6 are pixel-identical to each other and differ from X11: 1-px gaps between the arcs and the lines at 9 o'clock (100,150) and 6 o'clock (150,300), the arc ends step off the lines at the other corners, and the red 0–90° pie edge has a kink where it meets the blue line.
      • Cause win/tkWinDraw.c DrawOrFillArc rounds the radial endpoints and uses GDI's Arc/Pie conventions (exclusive right/bottom), as described in result-48.
      • Prototype none
      • Suggested ticket comment The full-circle part is fixed. The 1-px endpoint mismatch is still there on Windows 11 in 8.6.18 and 9.1: gaps and steps where the arcs of a rounded rectangle meet the lines. X11 is exact.
      2026-10-25 prototype, Windows VM
      • Status PROTOTYPED
      • Cause win/tkWinDraw.c:DrawOrFillArc passes radial points to Arc/Pie/Chord that are rounded points on the ellipse, measured from x+width/2. GDI measures the rays from the centre of its rectangle, which is one pixel larger (x+(width+1)/2), so the rays are tilted by up to half a pixel. Depending on the quadrant, an end stops one pixel short (a gap next to the line) or overshoots by one pixel. Arc also never draws its last pixel, like LineTo.
      • Fix compute the radial points from GDI's centre, 32× the size away, so that rounding them no longer tilts the ray. Move both ends inwards by a quarter pixel, clamped to a quarter of the extent so that tiny arcs never become full ellipses, because GDI includes the next pixel when the ray passes exactly through a pixel centre. For solid 1-px GXcopy outlines, also draw the last pixel. 1081603fff.patch (72 changed lines: 36 tkWinDraw.c, 36 test). It applies together with 1055974fff.patch (offsets only).
      • Repro work-win/arcs.tcl (rounded square plus pie) compared with the X11 capture (shots/arcs_x11.png). Before: gaps at 9 o'clock (100,149) and 6 o'clock (149,300). After: all 8 arc/line joints are closed, and the pie start no longer sticks out under the blue line. The rest of the difference from X11 (about 110 px, where the curve leaves the axes) comes from GDI's ellipse rasterisation and is not fixable without our own rasteriser. Tiny extents (0.05°–2°, both directions, arc and pieslice) are still drawn as tiny arcs, not full circles.
      • Test canvas-27.3 "arc ends neither stop short nor overshoot" (8 quarter arcs, testpixel: at each end, the pixel before or at the end is drawn and the one after is not) (fails before: yes, 8 ends on Windows main; passes after: yes; also passes on X11 with the wt-W build)
      • Test files run canvas 176/0, canvRect 57/0, canvPs 14/0, canvText 100/0, canvImg 84/0, canvWind 5/0 with fix; main canvas 175/0, the others identical
      • Windows done. Not checked: dashed and wide arcs, which are visually unchanged in the capture but have no pixel test, and stippled arcs, which are covered by canvas-27.1/27.2 and pass.
      • Suggested ticket comment The 1-px gaps and overshoots come from DrawOrFillArc: its radial points are measured from a centre half a pixel away from the one GDI uses, and GDI also skips the last pixel of Arc. A prototype aims the rays from GDI's centre and draws the last pixel, so arcs now meet the lines of a rounded rectangle exactly (test canvas-27.3). The remaining differences from X11 are GDI's ellipse rasterisation.

      • Tested not run on X11, where -dashoffset goes through XSetDashes and works. Windows repro script: win/1055974fff.tcl.       • Cause xlib/xgc.c:XSetDashes stores gc->dash_offset, but win/tkWinDraw.c:SetUpGraphicsPort never reads it. It also maps any dash list onto one of the fixed styles PS_DASH, PS_DOT, PS_DASHDOT or PS_DASHDOTDOT, so neither the offset nor the real dash lengths are honoured.

That changes the documented Windows dash behaviour in Tk_GetDash.3 and needs Windows to verify, so it is beyond a quick prototype.
      • Suggested ticket comment Still valid: XSetDashes stores the dash offset, but ''SetUpGraphicsPort'' in tkWinDraw.c ignores it and only picks one of the four stock pen styles. The fix is a PS_USERSTYLE pen made from the real dash list, rotated by the offset.
      Windows VM, run 5
      • Platform Windows
      • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip): offset 0 and offset 8 give identical captures at widths 1 and 3. The dash lengths are also wrong: {8 8} is drawn as the stock PS_DASH pattern at width 1, and as dots at width 3.

It builds a PS_USERSTYLE geometric pen from the real dash list, repeated twice when the list has an odd length, as in X11, and rotated by the offset. A 0-length entry is inserted when the pattern would start in an "off" segment. Dashed lines always use a geometric pen, because cosmetic pens count user styles in device style units (3 px on this display) and zero entries made them solid. Lists that would need more than 16 entries (odd lists longer than 7) fall back to the old mapping. Result: all 26 lines are pixel-identical to X11. Only the phase along the rectangle and oval outlines differs slightly. canvas, canvRect, canvPoly, canvLine, canvArc, canvText and winDraw tests: 312 run, 0 failed. No automated test (it needs screen capture).

      ⬜917631ffff2004Grid information in wm may be incorrect (patch included)REPRODUCED (main, 8.6). This is a design limitation.

      Open ticket 917631ffff · 48. Geometry Management · filed 2004-03-16

      Full verdict

      REPRODUCED (main, 8.6). This is a design limitation.

      • Platform generic (X11 tested)
      • Tested main, 8.6 tip on Xvfb without a WM. A listbox -setgrid 1 -height 10 and a text widget are packed with -expand 1 -fill both. After wm geometry . 20x20, wm geometry reports 20x20 grid units, but the listbox only shows about 15 lines because the text widget takes part of the extra space.
      • Cause Tk_SetGrid (tkUnixWm.c UpdateSizeHints, and the same on Windows and Aqua) derives the base size from requested sizes. The code assumes the gridded widget absorbs all size changes, and wm.n does not say so.
      • Prototype none. The attached patch adds a new public API (Tk_UpdateGrid) and changes UpdateSizeHints on all three platforms, so it is too large and changes the API. fvogel reported it did not help on Windows. Related: 3466588fff, 7c51624a96. A cheap alternative is a wm.n note that the gridded widget should be the only one that expands.
      • Suggested ticket comment Still present: gridding assumes the gridded widget gets all extra space. The proposed Tk_UpdateGrid needs new API (a TIP). Meanwhile, documenting this assumption in wm.n (GRIDDED GEOMETRY MANAGEMENT) would help.

      ⬜770578ffff2003long text item won’t scrollpatchREPRODUCED (main fb166f9f73, 9.0.5, 8.6.18; both Xft and --disable-xft builds)

      Open ticket 770578ffff · 05. Canvas Items · filed 2003-07-13

      Full verdict

      REPRODUCED (main fb166f9f73, 9.0.5, 8.6.18; both Xft and --disable-xft builds)

      • Platform X11 (generic canvas code)
      • Tested Xvfb, no WM. Test script with a non-periodic text ("00000|00001|..." in Courier 12) plus a short red reference item placed where the visible word should be. Scroll offsets 20000/40000/70000/140000. Up to about 32k the text is correct. At 40000 the long item shows 00547 where 00668 belongs, so it is shifted by 40000-32768 px. This happens on installed main, 9.0.5, 8.6.18 and the wt build, Xft and core fonts alike. The report's "bug!_" string repeats every 5 characters, which hides the shift; that is why the 2026-09-22 comment saw it as working with Xft.
      • Cause generic/tkCanvText.c:DisplayCanvText() gets the text origin from Tk_CanvasDrawableCoords(), which clamps it to a short. Once the item starts more than 32768 px left of the drawable, the whole layout is drawn from -32768. Separately, with core X fonts unix/tkUnixFont.c:Tk_DrawChars() passes x to XDrawString() as INT16, so a run starting below -32768 wraps around and could be drawn in the wrong place.
      • Prototype 770578ffff.patch (104 changed lines, mostly comments and two small static helpers). DisplayCanvText computes the origin as an unclamped int (DrawableCoord()), and the cursor/selection polygon points are clamped with ClampShort() instead of a wrapping (short) cast. Tk_DrawChars skips the XDrawString* call (but still advances x) for runs starting outside the 16-bit range. Checked by screenshot: the item is correct at all four offsets in both Xft and core-font builds. canvText.test, canvas.test, font.test and unixFont.test pass on the Xft build. The core-font build gives the same 3 unixfont-2.x failures with and without the patch (font availability). No new test: it would need pixel checks on a 40000-px item.
      • Suggested ticket comment The text is not only clipped but drawn shifted past 32k: DisplayCanvText() takes its origin from Tk_CanvasDrawableCoords(), which clamps to a short (the report's periodic string hides this). A prototype computes the origin without clamping and, for core X fonts, does not send runs starting below -32768 to XDrawString().
      Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Linux
      • Status VERIFIED
      • Applies to main clean
      • Repro new pixel-comparison script work-a3/p770.tcl (Courier 12 "00000|00001|..." canvas scrolled by S px, compared with a short reference item; count of differing pixels): before S=20000:0, 40000:1030, 70000:880, 140000:1051 / after all 0 (Xft build)
      • Test none (needs a pixel comparison on a canvas scrolled by more than 32k px; no portable read-back)
      • Test file run canvText.test 100/0, canvas.test 175/0, font.test 304/0, unixFont.test 50/0
      • Patch changes none. Review: DrawableCoord duplicates the static RoundCoord from tkCanvUtil.c, which is acceptable. Skipping core-font runs that start below SHRT_MIN is safe because Tk_DrawChars splits runs at about 200 chars, so a skipped run never reaches the visible area. Runs starting above SHRT_MAX are not skipped (asymmetric), but they wrap to positions far to the left, which are off-screen anyway. The core-font build was not rebuilt here (triage checked it).
      Prototype verified 2026-10-24, Windows VM, generic patches
      • 770578ffff: generic part builds; canvText 102/0 (the unix/tkUnixFont.c part does not apply to Windows).

      Open ticket 742773ffff · 48. Geometry Management · filed 2003-05-24

      Full verdict

      REPRODUCED (9.1 main: 113 frames; 8.6 tip: 110 frames with winfo id 0)

      • Platform Windows
      • Tested pack version, main + 8.6 on X11 (Xvfb, no WM): all 10000 frames mapped and placed correctly.
      • Cause (likely): Windows limits each process to 10000 USER objects by default (USERProcessHandleQuota). 80x125 frames plus their parents and the toplevel go over that. TkpMakeWindow (win/tkWinWindow.c) doesn't check whether CreateWindowExW() returned NULL, so the extra widgets get a NULL HWND and draw through the desktop DC. That's why they show up in the top-left corner of the screen. At most Tk could report an error; it can't create more windows than the OS allows.
      • Repro win/742773ffff.tcl (counts widgets whose winfo id is 0)
      • Prototype none (OS limit; only improvement would be an error from TkpMakeWindow on a NULL HWND)
      • Suggested ticket comment Not reproducible on X11. On Windows this is most likely the per-process limit of 10000 USER objects: the extra child windows can't be created. Tk doesn't check the result of CreateWindowExW(), so they end up drawing on the desktop.

      Open ticket 531658ffff · 69. Events · filed 2002-03-19

      ⏳ Since triage (updated 2026-10-31)

      Status now: Pending (Works for Me)

        Full verdict

        REPRODUCED on X11 (main, 9.0, 8.6, 8.5, 8.4) with eserte's example (grab set in the <ButtonPress-1> binding). The original Windows case (grab set before the press) is not reproduced: serhiy on Win11, and on X11 it goes through Note 6.

        • Platform generic (original report Windows; eserte's variant is X11)
        • Tested main (fb166f9f73), 9.0, 8.6 tip, 8.5, 8.4.20 on Xvfb+twm, with real XTest events: press on .b, drag to the root window, release. "release" is never printed, .b stays sunken and grab current is still "."; the ButtonRelease goes to "." (bind . <ButtonRelease-1> sees it at 598,571).
        • Cause generic/tkGrab.c. When a local grab is set while a button is down, Tk_Grab calls ReleaseButtonGrab (buttonWinPtr = NULL) and turns the grab into a temporary global XGrabPointer on the grab window, which replaces the X implicit grab of the pressed window. TkPointerEvent then delivers the release to the grab window by design ("Note 5": "forget about the auto-grab for the button press"), not to the window that got the press.
        • Prototype none. A fix would set dispPtr->buttonWinPtr to the pressed window in Tk_Grab when buttons are down. That needs the press window recorded outside grabs, and it changes where menus see the release (tk::MbPost/tk_popup set grabs with the button down), so it is a behaviour change to review carefully, not a quick patch.
        • Suggested ticket comment The Windows case from the report no longer reproduces. eserte's example (grab set from the ButtonPress binding) still loses the ButtonRelease on X11 in all versions: the release goes to the grab window, because Tk_Grab() deliberately forgets the button's implicit grab (tkGrab.c, Note 5). Workaround: also bind <ButtonRelease-1> on the grab window.

        Full verdict

        REPRODUCED (main, by instrumentation; the code path is the same in 8.6/8.5/8.4). No X error is visible here because only one X server is available: a second connection to the same server via -screen unix/:122.0 shares the XIDs, which hides the error.

        • Platform X11
        • Tested main, 8.6, 8.5, 8.4 with the ticket script on -screen :122.1 and unix/:122.0; no X error. With a temporary check in Tk_DrawChars on main, fontPtr->display != display fires twice after font delete; font create, and does not fire with the fix.
        • Cause generic/tkFont.c UpdateDependentFonts() reloads every cached instance of the named font through TkpGetFontFromAttributes(fontPtr, tkwin, ...) with the window that ran font create/configure. Instances allocated for another screen/display are therefore reopened on the wrong display (wrong font XID/XftFont and wrong DPI), which gives BadFont on a real second server. font configure of a named font takes the same path.
        • Prototype 483387ffff.patch (34 lines, no test: needs a second X server). Adds a FindWindowOnScreen() helper that finds a window on fontPtr->screen (falling back to tkwin) and passes it to TkpGetFontFromAttributes. font.test passes.
        • Suggested ticket comment Still there: UpdateDependentFonts() reloads font instances for other displays through the window that ran font create/configure, so they end up on the wrong display. Branch reloads each instance with a window on its own screen.

At 96 DPI (100%), -10/-11, -14/-15, -18/-19 and -22/-23 coll…
        Full verdict

        REPRODUCED (main, 8.6, 8.4)

        • Platform X11 (generic rounding)
        • Tested Xvfb at tk scaling 1.389: font actual {times -12} and {times -13} both give -size 9, while reqwidth (8.6: 40 vs 43) and linespace (13 vs 14) differ.
        • Cause generic/tkFont.c (around lines 3631/3696): font actual turns the actual pixel size into points and rounds to an integer ((int)(size + 0.5)). 12 px = 8.64 pt and 13 px = 9.36 pt both round to 9. fa.size is a double internally.
        • Prototype none. The fix changes the API: report a fractional point size, or report negative pixels when pixels were requested. That needs a design decision, maybe a TIP.
        • Suggested ticket comment Still present. font actual rounds the size to whole points, so -12 and -13 px both show as 9 pt at 100 dpi. Fixing it means reporting fractional points or pixels.
        ⬜220776ffff2000update idletasks does not update repacked widgets on WindowsREPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

        Open ticket 220776ffff · 69. Events · filed 2000-11-01

        Full verdict

        REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)

        • Platform Windows
        • Tested X11/Xvfb without a WM, main (9.1b1) and 8.6.18. An automated pixel check (PIL screen grab after each pack -anchor ...; update idletasks) found no stale button pixels; a sanity run confirmed that the check detects the button.
        • Cause win/tkWinWindow.c XMoveResizeWindow uses MoveWindow(..., TRUE), which only invalidates the uncovered area of the container. Windows then sends WM_PAINT, which becomes an Expose event, which schedules the idle redraw. [update idletasks] never processes window events, and Tk ignores WM_ERASEBKGND (tkWinX.c), so nothing repaints the container until the event loop runs. X11 is not affected because the X server fills uncovered areas with the window background itself.
        • Prototype none. A fix would need Windows paint messages handled synchronously inside [update idletasks], or Tk to erase backgrounds itself. That is a redesign and cannot be tested here. dkf (2004) considered the current behaviour acceptable; the workaround is [update] or timer-driven code.
        • Windows repro win/220776ffff.tcl (interactive: animates the repack, asks yes/no, prints the verdict and appends it to 220776-result.txt)
        • Suggested ticket comment Still Windows-only. On X11 the server repaints the uncovered area, so it does not occur there (checked with 9.1b1 and 8.6.18). On Windows the uncovered area is repainted only through WM_PAINT → Expose, which [update idletasks] does not process.
        Windows VM, run 4
        • Platform Windows
        • Verdict REPRODUCED (9.1 main, 8.6 tip)
        • Tested 220776_auto.tcl, the ticket script with screen shots while Tcl is blocked in after 700. The red area grows from 1.7 to 7.4 button areas. The final shot shows 9 red "Hello World!" copies (one at each anchor) on the blue background.
        • Cause moving the child window invalidates the parent area it uncovered. On Windows this only reaches Tk as WM_PAINT, which becomes a queued Expose event (win/tkWinX.c GenerateXEvent). update idletasks processes neither the message nor the window event, and WM_ERASEBKGND does nothing (tkWinX.c: result 0). On X11 the server repaints the window background by itself, so the problem does not show there. It is not a flushing problem (the 3017005 check shows GDI output appears at once), so a GdiFlush in XSync would not help.
        • Prototype none. A fix would mean either erasing with the Tk background pixel in WM_ERASEBKGND plus RedrawWindow(RDW_ERASENOW) in the XSync stub, or processing paint/Expose in update idletasks. That is a behaviour change, and there is a flicker risk.
        • Suggested ticket comment Still present on Windows 11 (8.6.18, 9.1): the areas a repacked widget uncovers are only repainted from WM_PAINT/Expose, which update idletasks does not process. X11 is unaffected because the X server paints window backgrounds.

        Full verdict

        REPRODUCED (main, 9.1, 8.6 tip, 8.6.10+, and partly 8.6.9, 8.6.8, 8.4.20). This contradicts the 2026-09-23 comment, which could not test a virtual root.

        • Platform X11 (virtual-root / reparenting window managers)
        • Tested I emulated a virtual-root WM on a WM-less Xvfb with a small Xlib helper (work-b4/vr/vroot.c). It creates a 3000x3000 window at -400,-300 with __SWM_VROOT, reparents Tk's wrappers into it, sets __SWM_ROOT on the wrappers and sends ICCCM synthetic ConfigureNotify. Three modes, probing points in ., in .t and on the desktop:
          • direct (wrappers are children of the vroot): all versions correct.
          • frames (one decoration frame per toplevel inside the vroot, as tvtwm/E do): OK in 8.4.20, 8.6.8, 8.6.9; broken in 8.6.10, 8.6.13–8.6.15, 8.6 tip and main ('' for both toplevels).
          • shared (several toplevels in one frame, the case raster describes): 8.4.20 and 8.6.8 find only .t; 8.6 tip and main find neither.
          • Normal WMs (twm, fvwm, icewm, no WM) work in every version.
        • Cause unix/tkUnixWm.c Tk_CoordsToWindow(), the child == wmPtr->reparent case, has two problems:
          • It calls PointInWindow(x, y, …) with coordinates relative to the previous level (the real root) rather than childX/childY (relative to the vroot, like winPtr->changes). This regression came with 359434f369 / 6942f5af38 (2019-01-04, fix for 18a4ba19bd), first released in 8.6.10.
          • It returns NULL as soon as the point is not in the first toplevel whose reparent window is hit. That is the old "one client per frame" assumption.
        • Prototype 220856ffff.patch (39 lines, ~10 changed). It uses childX/childY, and when the point is not in that toplevel it goes on with the other toplevels and then descends into the frame; title-bar points still give ''. Checked in all three modes, including a title-bar point, and with twm/fvwm/icewm; unixWm.test and winfo.test pass. No automated test: one needs an external reparenting client. The emulator could become a testvroot command if wanted.
        • Suggested ticket comment Reproduced by emulating a virtual-root WM. Since 8.6.10, winfo containing returns "" for any toplevel inside a decoration frame inside a virtual root, because Tk_CoordsToWindow() checks the point with coordinates relative to the wrong window. In all versions it also fails when the WM puts several toplevels into one frame. A small fix is ready.

        • 🔧 2026-10-04 check-in 9c5a5ddf6c on core-9-0-branch, in main, 9.0: Fix 22d2341c9d: Building extensions for Tk 8 and Tcl 8 in a Tcl 9 environment
        • 💬 2026-10-04 serhiy.storchaka: Since the fix for Tcl ad08bca6b1 (Tcl 9.0.4), tk.h of Tk 9.0 no longer compiles in Tcl 8 mode: tcl.h undefines Tcl_Size at its end, but tk.h and tkDecls.h use it. Proposed fix in branch tk8-mode-tcl-size: tk.h defines and undefines Tcl_Size as tclOODecls.h does. With it, the structures have the same sizes as in Tk 8.6. Tk 9.1 headers do not support Tcl 8 mode, so this is only for 9.0.
        • 💬 2026-10-04 jan.nijtmans: For Tk, "building with Tcl 8" actually meant "building with Tcl 8.7". Reason: Tk 9.0 uses Tcl_GetIntForIndex(), Tcl_CreateObjCommand2(), Tcl_DStringToObj() and Tcl_UtfToChar16DString(). Tk 9.0 was never meant to work with Tcl 8.6. Also, Tk 9.0 can only be compiled with TCL_UTF_MAX=4, in order to be able to use unicode codepoints > 0xFFFF. Tcl 8.6 doesn't support that at all. Tcl 8.7 does, but will never be release…
        • 💬 2026-10-04 jan.nijtmans: Hm, I was a little bit too quick. Something which is supported with Tk 9.0 is used its headers to compile actually with Tcl 8.6/Tk 8.6. In this situation, the tk8-mode-tcl-size makes sense. Thanks!
        Full verdict

        PARTLY (9.0 branch; main N/A). The original request is fixed: e754018763 + 055d75f68c (trunk, before 9.0.1, 2024-12-21) and fa41fdc007 (core-8-branch); TEA tclconfig c34cded0a3, then 18e57a1c95 (2025-04-08: --with-tcl8 also defines TK_MAJOR_VERSION=8). The rules.vc OPTS=tk8 was later dropped again (effef6c657), and trunk tk.h now has #error for TK_MAJOR_VERSION != 9. However, the 9.0 headers have regressed since Tcl 9.0.4.

        • Platform generic (build)
        • Tested compiled a file that includes only tk.h with -DTCL_MAJOR_VERSION=8 -DTK_MAJOR_VERSION=8 -DUSE_TK_STUBS (what TEA --with-tcl8 passes). Against Tk 9.0 branch tk.h:
          • Tcl 9.0.3 headers: compiles; sizeof Tk_CanvasTextInfo = 88 and Tk_OptionSpec = 72, the same as the real Tk 8.6 headers.
          • Tcl 9.0.4 headers (and core-9-0-branch): 109 errors, starting with tk.h:197: unknown type name 'Tcl_Size'.
        • Cause Tcl fix ad08bca6b1 / 7c1522f896 (Apr 2026, in Tcl 9.0.4). In Tcl 8 mode tcl.h now does #define Tcl_Size int and then #undef Tcl_Size at its end (_TCLSIZEHANDLED), but Tk 9.0 generic/tk.h, tkDecls.h and tkPlatDecls.h use Tcl_Size after #include <tcl.h>.

In Tcl 8 mode it defines Tcl_Size after tcl.h and undefines it at the end of tk.h, the same way tclOODecls.h does. Verified: compiles with Tcl 9.0.3 and 9.0.4 headers in 8 mode (same struct sizes as 8.6), and the normal 9.0 compile is unchanged.
        • Suggested ticket comment The --with-tk8 part works since 9.0.1 / tclconfig 18e57a1c95. However, since Tcl 9.0.4 (ad08bca6b1), Tk 9.0's tk.h no longer compiles in Tcl 8 mode: tcl.h undefines Tcl_Size, which tk.h still uses. Doing the same _TCLSIZEHANDLED dance in tk.h fixes it; after that the ticket can be closed (9.1 headers deliberately no longer support Tk 8).

          ⏳c2483bfe4b2020tk fontchooser on macOS can automatically open on startup, can lead to crashespatchPARTLY (read trunk, core-9-0-branch, core-8-6-branch; the macOS behaviour itself was not tested)

          Open ticket c2483bfe4b · 33. Generic Dialog Support · filed 2020-09-19

          ⏳ Since triage (updated 2026-10-31)

          Status now: Pending (Fixed)

            Full verdict

            PARTLY (read trunk, core-9-0-branch, core-8-6-branch; the macOS behaviour itself was not tested)

            • Platform Aqua
            • Tested Read macosx/tkMacOSXInit.c and macosx/tkMacOSXDialog.c on trunk, 9.0 and 8.6.
              • The reopening is fixed by 489304eaa9 (2020-10-20, merged to 8.6 and 8.7). closePanels() runs from TkpExitProc, which unix/tkAppInit.c installs through TkpWantsExitProc, and SIGINT/SIGHUP/SIGTERM handlers call Tcl_Exit. All of this is still on trunk.
              • The crash guard Marc described (a check for a NULL Tk_Window in TkSendVirtualEvent) is not in the code anymore. In FontchooserParseConfigure (-font case), if ([fp isVisible]) Tk_SendVirtualEvent(fcdPtr->parent, ...) still runs with fcdPtr->parent == NULL when this interpreter never ran tk fontchooser show. Tk_SendVirtualEvent then dereferences the NULL window (Tk_Display(target)).
              • This can still happen. The exit cleanup only covers wish started from a tty, and the signal handlers are skipped when the host (Python) already has its own. Another interpreter in the same process showing the shared NSFontPanel triggers it as well, e.g. interp create x; load {} Tk x; x eval {tk fontchooser show}; tk fontchooser configure -font Courier.
              • The FontchooserSelection branch has the same unguarded send. It is only reached with fontchooserInterp set, but fcdPtr->parent can be NULL after the parent window is destroyed.
            • Cause macosx/tkMacOSXDialog.c:FontchooserParseConfigure and FontchooserEvent send TkFontchooserFontChanged to fcdPtr->parent without checking that it is set. The parent is only set by show or -parent, but the NSFontPanel is shared and can be visible without that.
            • Prototype c2483bfe4b.patch (30 lines, against trunk: skips both sends when fcdPtr->parent is NULL; not compiled, no Mac)
            • Suggested ticket comment The startup reopening is fixed by 489304eaa9, but the reported segfault path is still there. tk fontchooser configure -font sends <<TkFontchooserFontChanged>> to a NULL parent whenever the shared font panel is visible without this interp having shown it (e.g. a second interp, or a non-wish host that skipped the exit cleanup). A two-line NULL check (prototype attached) would let this ticket be closed.

              ⬜3001403fff2010Wish crash on Windows XP when trying to free a colorpatchNEEDS palette mode (not testable: Windows 11 has no 8-bit desktop mode). Both prototypes compile cleanly with MSVC.

              Open ticket 3001403fff · 68. Win Window Operations · filed 2010-05-14

              Full verdict

              NEEDS palette mode (not testable: Windows 11 has no 8-bit desktop mode). Both prototypes compile cleanly with MSVC.

              • Platform Windows
              • Tested code inspection only. win/tkWinColor.c:XFreeColors still calls Tcl_Panic("Tried to free a color that isn't allocated").
              • Cause XFreeColors decides by the *current* RASTERCAPS & RC_PALETTE. A color allocated while the display was true-color has no refCounts entry. If the display switches to a palette mode (a full-screen game), freeing that color panics.
              • Prototype 3001403fff.patch (9 lines): skip pixels without a refcount entry and drop the second panic. Not compiled (no mingw here), no test.
              • Suggested ticket comment XFreeColors panics when the display switches to palette mode after colors were allocated in true-color mode. Freeing an unknown pixel can simply be ignored instead of panicking.
              Windows VM, run 4
              • Platform Windows
              • Verdict NEEDS palette mode (not testable: Windows 11 has no 8-bit desktop mode). Both prototypes compile cleanly with MSVC.
              • Tested each patch built in tk-wt91 (together with 2100553fff.patch). color.test, winfo.test, imgPhoto.test, frame.test, button.test, canvas.test, winDraw.test with 1708823: 1120 tests, 0 failed.
              • Cause XFreeColors decides by the current RC_PALETTE, while XAllocColor decided at allocation time
              • Prototype keep 1708823fff.patch (89 lines, mostly re-indentation). It drops the per-call GetDC/RC_PALETTE check and relies only on the refCounts table. That handles both directions: true-color → palette (no panic) and palette → true-color (entries are still freed, no leak). 3001403fff.patch only fixes the first direction and keeps the per-call GetDC. 3001403fff can be closed as a duplicate of 1708823.
              • Suggested ticket comment XFreeColors uses the current display mode to decide whether a pixel came from the palette. Using only the refcount table (as XAllocColor records it) fixes the panic after a mode switch. Fix in the 1708823 prototype.

              ⬜1708823fff2007Wish crash on Windows XP when trying to free a colorpatchNEEDS palette mode (not testable: Windows 11 has no 8-bit desktop mode). Both prototypes compile cleanly with MSVC.

              Open ticket 1708823fff · 29. Colormaps and Visuals · filed 2007-04-27

              Full verdict

              NEEDS palette mode (not testable: Windows 11 has no 8-bit desktop mode). Both prototypes compile cleanly with MSVC.

              • Platform Windows
              • Tested source review of main win/tkWinColor.c. XAllocColor and XFreeColors each check GetDeviceCaps(dc, RASTERCAPS) & RC_PALETTE separately, at the time they are called. So a color allocated in true-color mode and freed after switching to palette mode is not in cmap->refCounts, and Tcl_Panic("Tried to free a color that isn't allocated") fires. The opposite switch leaks palette entries. Repro script: win/1708823fff.tcl (needs a real depth switch while it waits).
              • Cause win/tkWinColor.c:XFreeColors decides by the current device mode instead of by how the pixel was allocated.
              • Prototype 1708823fff.patch (about 35 lines changed, mostly re-indentation; no test because it cannot be exercised automatically). XFreeColors no longer checks RC_PALETTE. It frees through the refCounts table whenever the pixel is found there. Palette pixels carry the PALETTERGB flag, so true-color pixels never match and are skipped instead of causing a panic. Not compiled (no mingw here).
              • Suggested ticket comment Still present in the code: XFreeColors checks the current palette mode rather than the mode used at allocation. The fix is to free through the refCounts table whatever the current mode is, and to ignore pixels not in the table.
              Windows VM, run 4
              • Platform Windows
              • Verdict NEEDS palette mode (not testable: Windows 11 has no 8-bit desktop mode). Both prototypes compile cleanly with MSVC.

              • Cause XFreeColors decides by the current RC_PALETTE, while XAllocColor decided at allocation time
              • Prototype keep 1708823fff.patch (89 lines, mostly re-indentation). It drops the per-call GetDC/RC_PALETTE check and relies only on the refCounts table. That handles both directions: true-color → palette (no panic) and palette → true-color (entries are still freed, no leak). 3001403fff.patch only fixes the first direction and keeps the per-call GetDC. 3001403fff can be closed as a duplicate of 1708823.
              • Suggested ticket comment XFreeColors uses the current display mode to decide whether a pixel came from the palette. Using only the refcount table (as XAllocColor records it) fixes the panic after a mode switch. Fix in the 1708823 prototype.
